      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He pointed her to a large chair covered with an antimacas- sar showcasing a pattern of rose garlands and quickly shoved off a couple of books that he’d left there. He grabbed his desk chair, sitting before her and lacing his hands together, leaning forward a little.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Do you get to see much of your family’s business?” he asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“When I was a kid I’d go to my father’s office and pretend to type reports and write memos. But I’m not so interested in that anymore.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You don’t want to be involved with it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“My brother loves it. But I don’t see why if my family has a paint company I should be in paint. Or worse: marry the heir of another paint company so we can have a larger company. Maybe I want to do something else. Maybe I have an amazing secret talent which must be exploited. You could be talking to a top-notch anthropologist here, you know.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Not a concert pianist, then.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Why not both?” she asked with a shrug.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Of course.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The chair was comfortable, and she liked his room. Noemi turned her head, looking at the watercolors of the mush- rooms. “Are those yours too?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. I did them a few years ago. They’re not very good.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“They’re beautiful.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“If you say so,” he replied, sounding dignified and smiling.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He had a plain face, mismatched even. She had liked Hugo Duarte because he was a pretty boy, and she appreciated a fellow with a certain slickness, who could dress well and play the game of charm. But she liked this man’s quirks and im- perfections, the lack of playboy smarts coupled with a quiet intelligence.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis was wearing his corduroy jacket again, but in the privacy of his room he walked around barefoot and had donned a rumpled old shirt. There was something lovely and intimate when he looked like this.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi was struck with the desire to lean forward and kiss him, a feeling like wishing to light a match, a burning, bright, and eager feeling. Yet she hesitated. It was easy to kiss some- one when it didn’t matter; it was more difficult when it might bemeaningful. eee</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_156" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 156 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182920_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She didn’t want to make a further mess of things. She didn’t want to play with him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You haven't come to compliment my drawings,” he said, as if he could sense her hesitation.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She hadn't. Not at all. Noemi cleared her throat and shook her head. “Have you ever thought your home might be haunted?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis gave her a weak smile. “That’s an odd thing to say.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m sure it is. But Ihave a good reason for asking. So, have your”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">There was silence. He slowly slid his hands into his pockets and looked down at the rug under their feet. He frowned.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I won't laugh at youif you tell me you've observed ghosts,” Noemi added.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There’re no such thing as ghosts.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“But what if there were? Have you ever wondered about that? I don’t mean ghosts under bedsheets, dragging chains behind them. I read a book about Tibet once. It was written by this woman called Alexandra David-Neel, who said people there were able to create ghosts. They willed them into exist- ence. What did she call them? Tulpa.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That sounds like a tall tale.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Of course. But there is this professor at Duke University, J. B. Rhine, who is studying parapsychology. Things like telep- athy as a kind of extrasensory perception.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What are you saying, exactly?” he asked, a terrible caution lacing his words.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m saying maybe my cousin is perfectly sane. Maybe there is a haunting in this house, but it can be explained logically. I don’t know quite how yet, maybe it’s got nothing to do with parapsychology, but take that old saying: mad as a hatter.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I don’t understand.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“People said hatters were prone to going crazy, but it was the materials they worked with. They inhaled mercury vapors when they made felt hats. You still have to be careful with that stuff nowadays. You can mix mercury into paints to control mildew, but under the right conditions the compounds give off sufficient mercury vapor to make people sick. You could have everyone in a room going mad and it’s the paint job.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis stood up suddenly and gripped her hands. “Don’t speak another word,” Francis told her, his voice low. He spoke in Spanish. They’d stuck to English since she’d arrived at the house; she didn’t recall him using one word of Spanish at High Place. She couldn’t remember him touching her either. If he</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_157" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 157 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182924_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">had, it hadn’t been deliberate. But his hands were steady on her wrists now.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Do you think I’m mad like those hatters?” she asked, also in Spanish.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Dear God, no. I think you're sane and clever. Much too clever, perhaps. Why won't you listen to me? Really listen. Leave today. Leave right this instant. This is no place for you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What do you know that you aren’t telling me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He stared at her, his hands still gripping her own. “Noemi, just because there are no ghosts it doesn’t mean you can’t be haunted. Nor that you shouldn’t fear the haunting. You are too fearless. My father was the same way, and he paid dearly for it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He fell down a ravine,” she said. “Or was there more to it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Who told you?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I asked a question first.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Acold pinprick of dread touched her heart. He shifted away from her, uneasily, and it was her turn to grip his hands. To hold him in place.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Will you speak to me?” she insisted. “Was there more to it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He was a drunk and he broke his neck, and he did fall down a ravine. Must we discuss this now?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. Because it seems you'll discuss nothing with me at any time.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That is not true. I’ve told you plenty. If you'd really listen,” he said, his hands extricating themselves from hers and rest- ing on her shoulders in a solemn motion.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’m listening.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He made a sound of protest, it was half a sigh, and she thought he might begin to talk to her, but then a loud moan echoed down the hall, and then another. Francis stepped away from her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The acoustics in this place, they were odd. It made her wonder why sound traveled so well.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s Uncle Howard. He’s in pain again,” Francis said, grim- acing, so that it almost looked like he was the one in agony. “He can’t hold on much longer.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m sorry. It must be difficult for you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You have no idea. If only he’d die.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was a terrible thing to say, and yet she imagined it must not be easy to live day after day in that creaky, musty house, walking on tiptoes so as to not upset the old man. What re- sentments could sprout in a young heart when all affection and love had been denied? Because she could not imagine any-</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_158" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 158 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182929_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">one ever loving Francis. Not his uncle, nor his mother. Had Virgil and Francis been friends? Did they ever look at each other, wearily, and confess their dissatisfactions? But Virgil, though perhaps also nursing his own grievances, had gone out into the world. Francis, he was tied to this house.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Hey,” she said, extending a hand to touch his arm.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T remember, when I was small, how he’d beat me with that cane of his,” Francis mused, his voice a hoarse whisper. “Teaching me strength,’ that’s how we put it. And I thought, dear Lord, Ruth was right. She was right. Only she couldn’t fin- ish him off. And there’s no point in trying, but she was right.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He looked so absolutely wretched, and although what he’d said had been terrible, she felt more pity than horror, and she didn’t flinch, her hand steady against his arm. It was Francis who turned his head away, who shirked her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Uncle Howard is a monster,” Francis told her. “Don’t trust Howard, don’t trust Florence, and don’t trust Virgil. Now you should go. I wish I didn’t have to send you off so quickly, but I should.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They were both quiet. He had his head down, his eyes lowered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I can stay for a bit, if you want me to,” she offered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He looked at her and smiled faintly. “My mother will havea fit if she finds you here, and she will be here any minute. When Howard is like this she needs us nearby. Go to sleep, Noemi.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“As if I could sleep,” she said with a sigh. “Although I could count sheep. Do you think that might help?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She ran a finger across the cover of a book that lay at the top of a pile, by the chair she had been occupying. She had nothing more to say and was simply delaying her departure, hoping he might speak to her more, despite his reservations; that he’d get to the matter of ghosts and a haunting that she wished to ex- plore, but it was no use.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He caught her hand, lifting it from the book, and looked down at her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Noemi, please,” he whispered. “I didn’t lie when I said they will come and fetch me.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He gave her back the oil lamp and held the door open for her. Noemi stepped out.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She looked over her shoulder before turning a corner. He seemed a bit ghostly, still standing by the doorway, with the glow of the lanterns and candles in his room lighting his blond hair like an unearthly flame. They said, in dusty little towns around the country, that witches could turn into balls of fire and fly through the air. That’s how they explained will-o’-the-</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_159" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 159 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182933_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">wisps. And she thought of that, and of the dream she’d had about a golden woman. 1 hr 11 mins left in book 56%</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_160" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 160 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182938_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">oemi hadn't been lying about counting sheep.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was too energized by all the thoughts of</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">hauntings, of answers to puzzles, to be lulled</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">into an easy slumber. And that moment when she’d thought to lean forward and plant a kiss on Francis’s lips was still bright in her mind, electric.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi decided that the best thing she could do was take a bath.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The bathroom was old, several of the tiles were cracked, but under the light of the oil lamp the tub appeared intact and decidedly clean, even if the ceiling was defaced by unsightly traces of mold.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi set the oil lamp on a chair and her bathrobe on the back of it, and opened the faucet. Florence had told her mild baths were what everyone ought to take, but Noemi didn’t in- tend to soak in a cold pool of water, and whatever issues the boiler might have, she was able to draw a hot bath for herself, the steam quickly filling the room.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Back home she would have sprinkled sweet-smelling oils or bath salts into the water, but there were none to be had. Noemi slipped into the bathtub anyway and rested her head back.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">High Place wasn’t exactly a dump, but there were so many small things wrong with it. Neglect. Yes, that was the right word. There was a great amount of neglect. Noemi wondered if Catalina might have turned things around, had the circum- stances been slightly different. She doubted it. Rot had set inin this place.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The thought was unpleasant. She closed her eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The faucet dripped a little. She sank deeper under the water until her head was completely underwater and she held her breath. When was the last time she’d gone swimming? She’d have to make a point to visit Veracruz soon. Better yet, Acapulco. She couldn't think of a place that would be more</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_161" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 161 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182943_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">different than High Place. Sun, beaches, cocktails. She could telephone Hugo Duarte and see if he was available.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">When she emerged, Noemi brushed the hair away from her eyes almost angrily. Hugo Duarte. Who was she kidding? She wasn't thinking about him these days. That arrow of yearn- ing that had struck her in Francis’s room was worrisome. It felt different from her other excursions into desire. Though a young woman of her social standing was not supposed to know anything about desire, Noemi had had the chance to ex- perience kisses, embraces, and certain caresses. That she did not sleep with any of the men she dated had less to do with a fear of sin than with the concern that they’d tattle about it to their friends, or worse, entrap her. There was always this smidgen of fear in her heart, fear of so many things, but with Francis she forgot to fear.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">You’re turning mawkish, she told herself. He’s not even hand- some.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She slid a hand up and down her breastbone and contem- plated the mold on the ceiling before sighing and turning her head away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">That’s when she saw it. The figure by the doorway. Noemi blinked, thinking for a moment it was an optical illusion. She'd brought the oil lamp into the bathroom and it provided enough light, but it wasn’t the stark illumination of a light bulb.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The figure stepped forward, and she realized that it was Virgil, in a navy pinstripe suit and a tie, looking nonchalant, as if he’d walked into his bathroom instead of her own.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There you are, you pretty little thing,” he said. “No need to speak, no need to move.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Shame and surprise and anger shot through her body. What the hell did he think he was doing? She was going to yell at him. She was going to yell at him and cover herself, and not only yell. She’d slap him. She’d slap him once she was in her bathrobe.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But she didn’t move at all. No sound escaped Noemi’s lips.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil stepped forward, a thin smile on his face.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They can make you think things, a voice whispered. She’d heard that voice before, somewhere in this house. They make you do things.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Her left hand was resting on the edge of the tub, and she managed, with considerable effort, to curl it tighter. She was able to open her mouth a little, but not to speak. She wanted to tell him to get out and couldn't, and it made her tremble with fright.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_162" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 162 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182947_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You'll be a good girl, won’t you?” Virgil said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He had reached the bathtub and knelt down to look at her, smiling. It was a cunning, crooked smile set in a perfectly sculpted face, and he was so close to her that she could see there were flecks of gold in his eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He tugged at the tie around his neck and took it off, then he unbuttoned his shirt.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was petrified, like the unwary character in an old myth. She was the victim of the gorgon.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Such a good girl, I know it. Be good to me.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Open your eyes, the voice said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But her eyes were wide open, and he had woven his fingers into her hair, making her lift her head up. A rough gesture, de- void of any of the kindness he was asking of her. She wanted to shove him away, but she still couldn’t move, and his hand clenched in her hair and he was leaning down to kiss her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi tasted sweetness on his lips. The trace of wine, per- haps. It was pleasant and it made her relax her tense body. She let go of the edge of the bathtub, and the voice that had been whispering to her was gone now. There was the steam from the bath and the man’s mouth atop her own, the hands snak- ing around her body. He kissed down her long neck, pausing to bite at the top of her breast, which drew a gasp from her. His stubble was rough against her skin.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Her neck arched backward. It seemed she could, in fact, move.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She raised her hands to touch his face, to draw him toward her. He wasn’t an intruder. He wasn’t an enemy. There was no reason to yell or to slap him, while there was every reason to keep touching him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">His hand ran down her stomach and disappeared beneath the water, caressing her thighs. She was not trembling with fright anymore. It was desire making her shiver, delicious and thick, spreading across her limbs, his touch heavy, his fingers toying with her as her breathing hitched. His body was hot against her skin. Another flick of his fingers, a deep exhal- ation, but then—</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Open your eyes, hissed the voice, yanking her hard, and she turned her face away from him, staring up again at the ceiling. The ceiling had melted away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She saw an egg, and from it rose a thin white stalk. A snake. But no, no, she’d seen such an image before. In Francis’s room a couple of hours ago. On the walls. The watercolors of mush- rooms with their neat labels beneath, and one of them had said “universal veil.” Yes. That’s what it was. The egg, pierced,</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_163" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 163 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182952_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">the membrane removed, the snake that was the mushroom rising through the ground. Alabaster snake, sliding and knot- ting itself, devouring its tail.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Then there was darkness. The light from the oil lamp had gone off. She wasn’t in the tub anymore. She had been wrapped in a thick cloth that impeded her movement, but she managed to pull it apart, to slide it away, and it slipped from her shoulders as neatly as the membrane she’d observed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Wood. She could smell damp earth and wood, and when she raised a hand her knuckles hit a hard surface anda splinter cut her skin.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Coffin. It was a coffin. The cloth was a shroud.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But she wasn’t dead. She wasn’t. And she opened her mouth to yell, to tell them that she wasn’t dead even when she knew she'd never die.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A buzzing, like a million bees had suddenly been un- leashed, and Noemi pressed her hands against her ears. A blinding golden light shivered; it touched her, moving from the tip of her toes up to her chest until it reached her face, smothering her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Open your eyes, Ruth said. Ruth with blood in her hands and blood on her face and her nails caked with blood, and the bees were inside her head, tunneling through Noemj’s ears.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi snapped her eyes open. Water was dripping down her back and her fingertips, and the bathrobe she was wearing was not cinched; it lay loose and open showing her nakedness. She was barefoot.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The room she stood in lay in shadows, but even in the dark the configuration indicated it was obviously not her own room. A dim lamp rose, like a firefly, grew brighter as nimble fingers adjusted it. Virgil Doyle, sitting in his bed, raised the lamp that had been resting at his bedside and regarded her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What’s this?” she asked, pressing a hand against her throat.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She could speak. Dear God, she could speak, even if her voice was hoarse and she was trembling.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I believe you managed to sleepwalk into my room.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was breathing much too quickly. She felt as though she had been running and God knew if she had. Anything was pos- sible. She managed to close her robe with a clumsy motion of her hands.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil pushed the covers away. He put on his velvet robe and approached her. “You're all wet,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I was taking a bath,” Noemi muttered. “What were you doing?”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_164" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 164 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_182957_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I was sleeping,” he said, reaching her side.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She thought he meant to touch her and took a step back, almost toppling the painted screen next to her. He steadied it with one hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T'll fetch you a towel. You must be cold.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Not that cold.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're a little liar,” he said simply and went rummaging in an armoire.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was not going to wait for him to find the towel. She meant to walk immediately back to her room, in absolute darkness if necessary. But the night had stunned her, it had reduced Noemi to a state of anxiousness that did not allow her to leave. As in the dream, she was petrified.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Here,” he said, and she clutched the towel for a minute, before finally drying her face and then slowly blotting her hair with it. She wondered how long she had been in the tub, and then how long she’d wandered down the hallways.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil slipped into the shadows, and she heard the clinking of glass. He returned with two glasses in his hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Sit and have a sip of wine,” he said. “It'll warm you up.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Let me borrow your lamp and I'll be out of here.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Have the wine, Noemi.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He sat in the same chair he’d used the last time, setting the oil lamp on a table, along with her drink, while he nursed his own glass. Noemi twisted the towel between her hands and sat down. She let the towel drop to the floor and picked up the glass, taking a sip—only one, as he’d suggested—very quickly, before setting the glass down again.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She felt as though she were still floating in the dream even though she had woken up. A haze lingered in her mind, and the only clear thing in the room was Virgil, his hair a little wild, his handsome face peering at her intently. He expected her to speak, that much was obvious, and she sought proper words.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You were in my dream,” she said. More for her sake than for his. She wanted to understand what she’d seen, what had happened.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I hope it wasn’t a bad dream,” he replied. He smiled. The smile was sly. It was the same smile she’d dreamed. Slightly malicious.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The ardor that she’d felt so vividly and pleasantly was now turning into a sour feeling in the pit of her stomach, but the smile was like a stray spark, reminding Noemi of her eager- ness, of his touch.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Were you in my room?”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_165" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 165 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183001_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I thought I was in your dream.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It did not feel like a dream.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What did it feel like?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Like an intrusion,” she said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I was sleeping. You woke me up. You are the intruder tonight.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She’d seen him rise from his bed and grab his velvet robe and yet she didn’t think him innocent. But he couldn’t have swept into the bathroom, like a medieval incubus, sitting on her chest as if they were posing for one of Fuseli’s paintings. Sneaking into her chamber to ravish her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She touched her wrist, wanting to feel the blue-and-white beads. She’d taken off the bracelet against the evil eye. Her wrist was bare. So was she, for that matter, wrapped in the white bathrobe, with water droplets still clinging to her body.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She stood up.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’ll be heading back now,” she declared.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You know, when you wake after sleepwalking you are not supposed to go back to bed right at once,” he said. “I really think you could use a little more wine.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No. I’ve had a terrible night and don’t wish to prolong it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Mmm. And yet if I didn’t agree to let you take my lamp you'd be forced to remain here for a few more minutes, wouldn’t you? Unless you plan to find your way back by touch- ing the walls. This house is very dark.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. I do plan to do that if you won't be polite and assist me.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I thought I was assisting you. I’ve offered you a towel to dry your hair, a chair to sit down, and a drink to calm your nerves.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“My nerves are fine.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He rose with the glass in one hand, eyeing her with a dry amusement. “What did you dream tonight?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She did not wish to blush in front of him. To turn crimson like an idiot in front of a man who wielded such meticulous hostility toward her. But she thought of his mouth on hers and his hands on her thighs, like it had been in the dream, and an electric thrill ran down her spine. That night, that dream, it had felt like desire, danger, and scandals, and all the secrets her body and her eager mind quietly coveted. The thrill of shamelessness and of him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She blushed after all.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil smiled. And even though it was impossible, she was sure that he knew exactly what she had dreamed, and that he was waiting for her to give him the smallest hint of an invi-</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_166" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 166 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183006_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">tation. The fog in her brain was clearing, though, and she re- membered the words in her ear. That single phrase. Open your eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi curled a hand into a fist, her nails digging into her palm. She shook her head. “Something terrible,” she said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil seemed confused, then disappointed. His face turned ugly as he grimaced. “Perhaps you were hoping to sleepwalk into Francis’s room, hmm?” he asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The words shocked her, but they also gave her the confi- dence to stare back at him. How dare he. And after he had said they could be friends. But she understood now. This man was an absolute liar, toying with her, attempting to confuse and distract her. He turned kind for a second when it suited him, granted her an inch of cordiality, then took it away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Go to sleep,” she said, but in her mind she thought fuck you, and her tone plainly indicated that. She snatched the lamp and left him in the shadows.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">When she reached her room she realized it had started to rain. The sort of rain that does not ease, a constant pat- ter against the window. She ventured into the bathroom and looked at the bathtub. The water was cold, and the steam had dissipated. She yanked up the plug.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_167" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 167 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183011_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">oemi slept fitfully, afraid she’d launch into an- other somnambulistic escapade. Eventually, she dozed off.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">There was a rustle of cloth in her room, the creak of a board, and she turned her head in fright toward the door, her hands clutching her bedsheets.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was Florence in another of her prim dark dresses and her pearls. She had let herself into her room and carried a silver tray in her hands.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What are you doing?” Noemi asked, sitting up. Her mouth felt dry.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tt’s lunchtime,” Florence said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It couldn't be that late, could it? Noemi got up and pulled the curtains aside. Light streamed in. It rained still. The morn- ing hours had burned away without her noticing, exhaustion bleeding her dry.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Florence set the lunch tray down. She poured a cup of tea for Noemi.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Oh, no, thanks,” Noemi said, shaking her head. “I wanted to see Catalina before eating.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“She’s woken up already and has gone back to bed,” Flor- ence replied, setting the teapot down. “Her medication is mak- ing her very sleepy.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“In that case, will you tell me when the doctor arrives, then? He is supposed to come today, isn’t he?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He won't be here today.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I thought he visited every week.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s still raining,” Florence said, indifferent. “He won't come up with this rain.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It might rain tomorrow too. After all, it’s the rainy season, isn’t it? What’ll happen then?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We'll get by on our own, we always have.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_168" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 168 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183015_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">What neat, crisp answers to everything! Why, it almost felt like Florence had written and memorized all the right things to say.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Please tell me when my cousin wakes up,” Noemi insisted.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’m not your servant, Miss Taboada,” Florence replied. Her voice lacked animosity, though. It was merely a fact.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“IT am well aware of that, but you demand that I not visit Catalina without warning and then you set up an impossible schedule for me. What is your problem?” she asked. She real- ized she was being incredibly rude, but she wished to draw a crack through Florence’s calm facade.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“If you have an issue with that, you'd best bring it up with Virgil.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil. The last thing she wished to do was bring anything up with Virgil. Noemi crossed her arms and stared at the woman. Florence stared back at her, her eyes very cold and her mouth curved a little, the slightest hint of derision.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Enjoy your lunch,” Florence concluded, and there was su- periority in her smile, as if she thought she’d won a battle.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi stirred the soup with her spoon and sipped the tea. She quickly gave up on both of them. She felt the beginning of a headache. She ought to eat but stubbornly decided to look around the house.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi grabbed her sweater and walked downstairs. Did she hope to find anything? Ghosts, peeking from behind doors? If there were any, they evaded her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The rooms with sheets on top of the furniture were dire, and so was the greenhouse with its wilted plants. Aside from evoking a mild sense of depression, they revealed nothing. She ended up seeking refuge in the library. The curtains were drawn, and she pulled them open.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She looked down at the circular rug with the snake she had noticed during her first visit and slowly walked around it. There had been a snake in her dream. It burst from an egg. No, from a fruiting body. If dreams had meaning, what did this one tell her?</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Well, she was damn sure one needn’t phone a psychoana- lyst to determine it had a sexual component to it. Trains going through tunnels make for neat metaphors, thanks, Mr. Freud, and apparently phallic mushrooms straining through the soil served the same purpose.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil Doyle straining against her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">That was no metaphor; it was crystal clear.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The memory of him, with his hands in her hair, his lips against her own, made her shiver. But there wasn’t anything</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_169" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 169 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183020_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">pleasant in the memory. It was cold and disturbing, and she turned her eyes toward the bookshelves, furiously looking among the tomes for a book to read.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi grabbed a couple of books at random and went back to her room. She stood by the window, looking outside, nib- bling on a nail before she decided she was too nervous and needed a smoke. She found the cigarettes, the lighter, and the cup decorated with half-naked cupids that she utilized as an ashtray. After taking a drag, she settled on the bed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She hadn’t even bothered to read the titles of the books she’d picked. Hereditary Descent: Its Laws and Facts Applied to Human Improvement, it said. The other book was more inter- esting, dwelling on Greek and Roman mythology.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She opened it and saw the faint, dark marks of mold upon the first page. She turned the pages carefully. The interior pages were mostly intact, a few tiny spots on a corner or two. They made her think of snatches of Morse code. Nature writ- ing upon paper and leather.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi held the cigarette in her left hand and let the ash drop into the cup, which she’d placed on the side table. Golden- haired Persephone, the book informed her, had been dragged down into the Underworld by Hades. There she ate a few seeds of pomegranate, which chained her to his shadow world.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The book contained an engraving showing the exact mo- ment when Persephone was snatched away by the god. Per- sephone’s hair was strewn with flowers and a few flowers had fallen to the ground; her breasts were bare. Hades, reaching from behind, had picked her up, clutching her in his arms. Per- sephone had one hand in the air and swooned, a scream on her lips. Her expression was one of horror. The god stared forward.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi clapped the book shut and looked away, her eyes landing on the corner in her room where the rose-colored wallpaper was stained black by mold. And as she looked at it, the mold moved.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Christ, what kind of optical illusion was that?!</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She sat in the bed and gripped the covers with one hand while with the other she held her cigarette. Slowly she stood and approached the wall, unblinking. The shifting mold was mesmerizing. It rearranged itself into wildly eclectic patterns that reminded her of a kaleidoscope, shifting, changing. In- stead of bits of glass reflected by mirrors it was an organic madness that propelled the mold into its dizzy twists and turns, creating swirls and garlands, dissolving, then remer- ging.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_170" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 170 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183024_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">There was color to it too. At first glance it appeared black and gray, yet the longer Noemi looked at the mold, the more it became obvious there was a golden sheen to certain sections of it. Gold and yellow and amber, dulling or intensifying as the patterns remade themselves into a new combination of stag- gering, symmetrical beauty.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She reached a hand up, asif to touch that section of the wall that was dirtied by the mold. The mold moved again, away from her hand, skittish. Then it seemed to change its mind. It pulsated, as if it was bubbling up, like tar, and it crooked a long, thin finger, beckoning her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">There were a thousand bees hiding in the walls, and she heard them buzzing as she pressed forward drowsily, intend- ing to slide her lips against the mold. She’d run her hands across the shimmering gold patterns, and they would smell of earth and green, of rain, and then they would speak a thou- sand secrets.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The mold beat to the rhythm of her heart; they beat as one, and her lips parted.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The forgotten cigarette, still in her possession, burned Noemi’s skin, and she let go of it with a yelp. She quickly bent down and picked up the cigarette, tossing it into her makeshift ashtray.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She turned around to look at the mold. It was absolutely still. The wall looked like old, dirty wallpaper and had not changed even a little bit.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi rushed into the bathroom and shut the door. She gripped the edge of the sink to keep herself steady. Her legs were about to fail her, and she thought, panicked, that she would faint.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She opened the faucet, splashing cold water against her face, unwilling to collapse even if it took all her damn might. Breathe and breathe again, that’s what she did.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“God damn it,” Noemi whispered, bracing herself with both hands against the sink. The dizzy spell was passing. But she wasn’t going out there. Not for a while, at least. Until she made sure...made sure of what? That she’d stopped hallucinating? That she wasn’t going mad?</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi slid one hand against her neck while she examined the other. She had a great, nasty burn between her index and middle fingers, where the cigarette had burned down toa stub. She’d have to obtain an ointment for that.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi splashed more water against her face and stared into the mirror, her fingertips on her lips.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A loud knock made her jump back.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_171" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 171 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183029_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are you in there?” Florence asked. Before Noemi had time to reply, the woman opened the door.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Give me a minute,” Noemi muttered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Why are you smoking when it’s forbidden?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi whipped her head up and scoffed at the inane ques- tion. “Yeah? I think the more important question is what the fuck is going on in this house?” Noemi said. She wasn’t quite yelling, but she was awfully close.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What language! Watch how you talk to me, young girl.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi shook her head and closed the faucet. “I want to see Catalina, right away.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t you dare order me around. Virgil will be here any minute and you'll see—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She clutched Florence’s arm. “Listen—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Take your hands off me!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi squeezed her fingers harder while Florence tried to push her away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What’s this?” Virgil asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He stood at the doorway, looking at them curiously. He had on the same pinstriped jacket that he’d worn in her dream. It gave her a jolt. She’d likely seen it on him before, which is why she’d pictured him wearing it in the first place, but she didn’t like this detail. It blended reality and fantasy together. It un- nerved her enough to release Florence.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“She’s been breaking the rules, as usual,” Florence said, carefully smoothing back her hair even though it did not need to be smoothed. As if their brief confrontation could have upset her well-coiffed head. “She’s a nuisance.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What are you doing here?” Noemi asked, crossing her arms.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You yelled, and I came to see if anything was amiss,” Virgil told her. “I imagine that’s the same reason why Florence is here.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Indeed,” Florence replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I didn’t yell for anyone.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We both heard you,” Florence insisted.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi had definitely not yelled. There had been noise, but that was the noise from the bees. Of course there were no bees, but that didn’t mean she had yelled. She would damn well re- member if she yelled. The cigarette had burned her hands, but she hadn’t made that much noise and—</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They both looked at her. “I want to see my cousin. Now. I swear to God, you let me see her or I’ll knock her door down,” she demanded.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil shrugged. “There is no need for that. Come.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_172" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 172 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183034_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She followed them. At one point Virgil looked at her over his shoulder and smiled. Noemi rubbed her wrist and looked away. When they walked into Catalina’s room she was sur- prised to see her cousin awake. Mary was also there. It seemed this would be a group reunion.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Noemi, what is it?” Catalina asked, a book in her hands.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I wanted to see how you were doing.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Same as yesterday. Resting, mostly. It seems I’m the Sleep- ing Beauty.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Sleeping Beauty, Snow White. Noemi couldn’t care less about that right now. But Catalina was smiling kindly, like she always used to smile. “You look tired. Anything wrong?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi hesitated and shook her head. “It’s nothing. Do you want me to read to your” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I was going to have a cup of tea. Do you want to join me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi wasn’t sure what she had expected to find, but it wasn't Catalina in high spirits, the maid quietly arranging flowers in a vase, the meager blooms from the greenhouse. The scene struck her as artificial and yet there was nothing wrong. She stared at her cousin, trying to find the faintest trace of discomfort in her face.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Really, Noemi. You seem a little odd. You aren't getting a cold, are you?” Catalina asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’m fine. I’ll let you have your tea,” Noemi said, unwilling to reveal more in the presence of the others. Not that they seemed terribly interested in this conversation.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She stepped outside. Virgil exited the room too and closed the door. They looked at each other.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are you satisfied?” he asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m appeased. For now,” she replied tersely, intending to walk back to her room alone, but he was going in the same dir- ection, obviously wishing to continue their conversation and not minding her curtness.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“And I thought there was no appeasing you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What’s that supposed to mean?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're on a quest to find faults around you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Faults? No. Answers. And let me tell you, they’re pretty big ones.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are they?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I saw this awful thing, moving—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Last night or now?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Now. And last night too,” she muttered, pressing her hand against her forehead.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_173" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 173 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183038_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She realized then that if she headed back to her room she’d have to look at the ugly wallpaper with the hideous black stain on it. She wasn’t ready to face it. Noemi changed course, quickly veering toward the stairs. She could always hide in the drawing room. It was the most comfortable room in the house.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“If you’re having bad dreams I can ask the doctor for a rem- edy to help you sleep the next time he visits us,” Virgil said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She walked faster, intent on putting distance between him and her. “That won’t do any good since I wasn’t dreaming.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You weren't dreaming last night? But you walked in your sleep.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She turned around. They were standing on the stairs, and he was three steps above her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That was different. Today I was awake. Today—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It all sounds very confusing,” he interjected.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That’s because you're not giving me a chance to speak.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're very tired,” he said dismissively as he began to des- cend those steps.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi went down three more steps, attempting to main- tain the same gap between them. “Is that what you told her? Youre very tired? Did she believe you?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A moment later he had reached Noemi and bypassed her, descending the final steps to the ground floor. He turned to look at her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I think it’s better if we leave it at this for now. You're agitated.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T don’t want to leave it at this,” she said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Oh?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil slid a hand over the shoulder of the carved nymph that grasped the newel post at the bottom of the staircase. A sordid spark danced in his eyes. Or was she imagining that too? Was there something else to that casual “oh,” to that smile spreading across his face?</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She descended the steps, giving him a challenging look. But then her courage evaporated when he leaned forward and she thought he was about to transfer his hand to her shoulder.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">In the dream there had been a strange taste in his mouth, like ripe fruit, and he, with the pinstriped jacket, hovering above her, taking off his clothes, slipping into the tub and touching her, while Noemi wrapped her arms around him. The memory was tinged with arousal, but also with a terrible humiliation.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">You'll be a good girl, won’t you? He’d told her that. And here they were now, wide awake, and she realized that he was cap- able of saying exactly that to her in real life. That he’d have</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_174" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 174 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183043_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">no trouble snidely delivering such a line, that his strong hands could find her in the daylight or the dark.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was afraid he’d touch her and of how she’d react. “I wish to leave High Place. Can you tell someone to drive me back to towne” she asked quickly.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're full of impulsiveness today, Noemi,” he said. “Why would you be leaving us?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I don’t need a reason.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She’d come back. Yes, that was right. Or even if she didn’t leave, if she could get as far as the train station and write to her father, it would all be better. The world seemed to be collaps- ing around her, becoming a confused mess, dreams bleeding into her waking hours. If she was able to step out, to discuss the strange experiences she was having at High Place with Dr. Camarillo, then maybe she’d feel like herself again. Camarillo might even be able to help her figure out what was going on, or what she should do. Air. She needed fresh air.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Of course not. But we can’t drive you back with all this rain. I told you, the roads are treacherous.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She could see the raindrops splattered against the colored glass window on the second landing. “Then I’ll walk back.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You'll drag your suitcase in the mud? Perhaps you intend to use it as a boat and paddle away on it? Don’t be silly,” he said. “The rain must cease today, and we can attempt the drive to- morrow morning. Will that suffice?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Now that he’d agreed to take her to town she was able to breath and unclench her tense hands. Noemi nodded.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“If you really are leaving us tomorrow, then you should have dinner with us one final time,” Virgil said, sliding his hand off the nymph and glancing down the hallway, in the dir- ection of the dining room.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Very well. And I’ll want to talk to Catalina too.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Of course. Is there anything else?” he asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No,” she said. “There’s nothing.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It wasn’t a lie, but she still avoided his gaze, and for a moment she remained unmoving, not knowing whether he might continue following her as she went toward the sitting room. But remaining wouldn't do her any good either.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She began walking.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Noemi?” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She paused to look back at him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Please don’t smoke again. It disturbs us,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t worry,” she replied and, remembering the cigarette burn on her hand, she looked at her fingers. But the red, raw mark was gone. There was no sign of it at all.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_175" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 175 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183048_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi held up her other hand, thinking that perhaps she’d mistaken which hand she’d injured. Nothing there either. She flexed her fingers and hurried to the sitting room, her steps loud as she walked. She thought she heard Virgil chuckle, but she wasn’t sure. She wasn’t sure of anything at all.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_176" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 176 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183052_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">oemi packed her suitcases slowly, feeling traitor-</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">ous and second-guessing herself. Yes. No. Per-</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">haps it would be best to remain. She truly did not</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">wish to leave Catalina alone. But she’d said she was going to town, and it was vital that she clear her head. She decided she wouldn't quite return to Mexico City. Instead she would journey to Pachuca, where she’d write to her father and find a good doctor willing to accompany her back to High Place. The Doyles would be reluctant to allow this, but it was better than nothing.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Emboldened, with a plan of attack, she finished her pack- ing and headed to dinner. Because it was her last night at High Place and because she didn’t wish to seem haggard or de- feated, she decided to wear a party dress. It was a buff-colored embroidered tulle dress with metallic gold accents, a yellow acetate bow at the waist, and a perfectly boned bodice. Not as full a skirt as she normally liked to wear, but very flattering and perfectly adequate for a dinner.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Obviously the Doyles had the same thought, treating this as an important, almost celebratory moment. The tablecloth of white damask was laid out, as were the silver candelabra, and a multitude of candles had been lit. In preparation for Noemi’s departure they had lifted the ban on conversation, though this evening she might have enjoyed the silence. Her nerves were still much too raw from the strange hallucin- ation she’d experienced. Even now Noemi wondered what had caused the bizarre episode.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was getting a headache. Noemi blamed the wine. It was strong and yet very sweet; it lingered on the palate.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The poor company did not make matters any better. She must pretend cordiality for a little bit more, but her patience had been stretched to its limits. Virgil Doyle was a bully and Florence wasn't any better.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_177" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 177 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183057_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She glanced in Francis’s direction. By her side sat the member of the Doyle family she appreciated. Poor Francis. He looked rather miserable that evening. She wondered if he’d drive her to town the next morning. She hoped so. It might give them time to speak in private. Could she trust him to take care of Catalina for her? She must ask for his help.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis eyed her back, a fleeting look. His lips parted to whisper a word before Virgil’s loud voice hushed him. “We'll venture upstairs after supper, of course.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi raised her head. She looked at Virgil. “I’m sorry?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I said my father expects us to all pay him a visit after sup- per. To say his goodbyes to you. You won’t mind a short trip to his room, will you?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“IT wouldn’t dream of leaving without saying goodbye,” she replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“And yet you most eloquently wished to walk your way to town a few hours ago,” Virgil said. The words had a mordant little twist.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">If she liked Francis, then she had decided she could not stomach Virgil. He was hard and unpleasant, and beneath that veneer of wretched civility she knew he could be beastly. Most of all she loathed the way he was looking at her now, which he’d done before, a chilling little smirk on his lips and his eyes fixed on her with a rawness that made her want to cover her face.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">In the dream, in the bathtub, she’d felt much the same. Yet there had also been another feeling running all through her. It was pleasurable, but in a terrible way, like when she’d had a cavity and kept pressing her tongue against it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A panting, ferocious, and sickly lust.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was a wicked thought to have at the dinner table, with him sitting across from her, and she looked down at her plate. This was a man who could know secrets, who could divine un- articulated desires. She must not look at him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A long silence stretched between them as the maid walked in and began taking away the dishes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You might have trouble getting into town come morning,” Florence said once more wine had been poured and dessert was set before them. “The roads will be terrible.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes, all these floods.” Noemi nodded. “That is how you lost the mine?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Ages ago,” Florence replied, waving a hand in the air. “Vir- gil was a baby.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil nodded. “It was waterlogged. Anyway, it’s not like it was being worked on. With the Revolution going on, you</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_178" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 178 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183101_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">couldn’t get nearly enough workers here. They’d all be fighting for one side or the other. You need a constant influx of workers at amine like this.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I suppose it was impossible to get people back after the Revolution ended? Had they all gone away?” Noemi asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes, and besides, we had no way to hire new crews, and my father was ill for a long time, so he couldn’t oversee the work. Of course, that’ll change soon.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“How so?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina hasn’t mentioned it? It is our intention to open up the mine again.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“But it’s been closed for a very long time. I thought your finances were strained,” Noemi protested.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina has decided to invest in it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You didn’t mention that before.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It slipped my mind.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He spoke so casually that one might be tempted to actually believe him. But Noemi was betting he had kept his lips tightly shut knowing the conclusion she would draw based on that: that Catalina was going to serve as a docile piggy bank.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">If he was speaking now, it was because he meant to rile her up a little, to throw in her direction that sharp smile he had deftly shown her on more than one occasion. He wished to gloat. Because she was going away, after all, so a little gloating couldn’t hurt now.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Is it very wise to do such a thing?” she asked. “With your wife in her condition?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It is not as if it’ll make her worse, don’t you think?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I think it’s callous.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We've long been simply existing at High Place, Noemi. Too long. It is now time to grow again. The plant must find the light, and we must find our way in this world. You may con- sider that callous. I find it natural. And, in the end, it was you who was speaking of change to me the other day.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">How lovely that he should pin this project on her. Noemi pushed her chair back. “Maybe I should say good night to your father now. I’m tired.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil held the stem of his glass and raised an eyebrow at her. “I suppose we could skip dessert.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Virgil, it’s much too early,” Francis protested.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He had spoken only those words that evening, but both Vir- gil and Florence turned their heads in his direction brusquely, as if he’d been saying offensive things all night long. Noemi guessed that he was not supposed to offer any sort of opinions. It did not surprise her.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_179" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 179 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183106_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’d say it’s about the right time,” Virgil replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They stood up. Florence led the way, taking an oil lamp that rested on a sideboard. The house was very chilly that evening, and Noemi crossed her arms against her chest, wondering if Howard would want to talk for long. Dear Lord, she hoped not. She wished to sneak under the covers and go to sleep as quickly as possible so that she might wake up early and jump into the blasted car.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Florence opened the door to Howard’s room, and Noemi followed her in. A fire was burning, and the curtains around the large bed were closed tight. There was an ugly smell in the air. Too pungent. Like a ripe fruit. Noemi frowned.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We are here,” Florence said, setting her oil lamp down on the mantelpiece above the fireplace. “We have your visitor.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Florence then went by the bed and began peeling the cur- tains away. Noemi schooled her features into a polite smile, ready for the sight of Howard Doyle tucked neatly under the covers or perhaps lounging against the pillows in his green robe.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She did not expect him to be lying there, over the blankets, naked. His skin was terribly pale and his veins contrasted gro- tesquely against his whiteness, indigo lines running up and down his body. Yet that was not the worst of it. One of his legs was hideously bloated, crusted over with dozens of large, dark boils.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She had no idea what they were. Not tumors, no, for they pulsed quickly, and their fullness contrasted with his emaci- ated body, the skin grown taut against the bones except upon that leg where the boils grew, as thick as barnacles upon a ship’s hull.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was horrid, horrid, and she thought he was a corpse, afflicted by the ravages of putrefaction, but he lived. His chest rose and dipped, and he breathed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You must get closer,” Virgil whispered into her ear and clasped her tight by the arm.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The shock had prevented Noemi from moving, but now that she felt his hand closing around her, she attempted to shove Virgil away and rush to the door. He yanked her back, though, with a vicious strength that threatened to snap her bones, and she gasped in pain, but still she fought him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Come on, help me here,” Virgil said, looking at Francis.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Let go of me!” she screamed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis did not approach them, but Florence grabbed Noemi’s free arm, and together Virgil and the woman dragged Noemi toward the head of the bed. She twisted her body and</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_180" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 180 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183112_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">managed to kick the night table, sending a porcelain chamber pot crashing onto the floor.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Kneel down,” Virgil ordered her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No,” Noemi said.</p>
