      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They shoved her down, Virgil’s fingers digging into her flesh, and he placed a hand behind her neck.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard Doyle turned his head upon the pillow and looked at her. His lips were as bloated as his leg, crusted with black growths, and a trail of dark fluid dripped down his chin, stain- ing his bedclothes. This was the source of the bad smell in the room, and up close the stench was so awful she thought she would retch.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“My God,” she said and tried to get up, to scuttle away, but Virgil’s hand was a band of iron around her neck, and he was pushing her even closer to the old man.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And the man was rising in his bed, turning and stretching out a thin hand, his fingers digging into Noemi’s hair and pull- ing their faces closer.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was able, at this disgustingly intimate distance, to clearly see the color of his eyes. They were not blue. The color was diluted by a bright, golden sheen, like flecks of molten gold.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard Doyle smiled at her, showing off his stained teeth —stained with black—and then he pressed his lips against hers. Noemi felt his tongue in her mouth and then saliva burn- ing down her throat as he pressed himself against her and Vir- gil propped her in place.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He let go of her after long, agonizing minutes, and Noemi was able to gasp and turn her head.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She closed her eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She felt very light; her thoughts were scattered. Drowsy. My God, she told herself, my God, stand up, run. Over and over again.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">When she looked around, she tried to focus her eyes and saw that she was in a cave. There were people there. A man had been handed a cup, and he was drinking from it. The hid- eous liquid burned his mouth, and he almost passed out, but the others laughed and they clasped his shoulder in a friendly manner. They hadn’t been so friendly when he’d first arrived, a stranger in these parts. They were skittish, and for good reason.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The man was fair-haired, and his eyes were blue. He shared a resemblance with Howard, with Virgil. The shape of the jaw, the nose. But his clothes and his shoes and everything about him and the men in the cave pointed to a previous time.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_181" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 181 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183117_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">When is this? Noemi thought. But she felt dizzy, and the sound of the sea distracted her. This cave, was the ocean nearby? The cave was dark; one of the men held a lantern, but it did not provide much illumination. The others continued with their jokes, and two of them helped the blond man up. He stumbled.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The man wasn’t doing very well, but that wasn’t their fault. He’d long been ill. His physician said there was no cure. There was no hope, but Doyle had hoped.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle. That was him, yes. She was with Doyle.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle was dying and in his desperation he’d found his way here, seeking a remedy for those who were beyond remedies. In- stead of a peregrination to a holy site, he’d come to this wretched cave.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They hadn’t liked him, no, but these folk were poor, and he had a fat purse of silver. Of course, he’d feared they’d cut his throat and take the silver, but what else was there to be done? All he could manage was to promise them there’d be more where that came from if they kept their end of the bargain.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Money wasn’t everything, of course. He knew as much. They recognized him as their natural superior. Force of habit, he im- agined. My lord spilled from their lips, even though these were scavengers.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">In the corner of the cave Noemi saw a woman. Her hair was stringy, her face plain and pasty. She held a shawl around her shoulders with a bony hand and looked at Doyle with interest. There was a priest too, an old man who tended to the altar of their god. For in the end this was indeed a holy site of a strange sort. Instead of candles, the fungus hanging from the cave walls, luminescent, lit a crude altar. Upon it there were a bowl and a cup and a pile of old bones.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">If he died, Doyle thought, his bones would be added to that pile. But he was not afraid. He was half dead already.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi rubbed a hand against her temples. A terrible head- ache was building inside her skull. She squinted, andthe room wavered, like a flame. She tried to focus on something and fixed her eyes on Doyle.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle. She’d seen him stumbling around, his face worn down by disease, but now he looked so hearty she almost confused him with another man. His vitality restored, one would have expected him to return home at once. But here he was lingering, running a hand down the woman’s naked back. They’d married, following the custom of her people. Noemi felt his disgust as he touched the woman, but he kept a smile on his face. He must dissimulate.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_182" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 182 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183121_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He needed them. Needed to be accepted, needed to be one with these rough folk. For only then could he know all their secrets. Eternal life! It was there for the taking. The fools didn’t under- stand it. They used the fungus to heal their wounds and preserve their health, but it could be so much more. He’d seen it, the evi- dence was in the priest they blindly obeyed, and what he hadn’t witnessed he’d imagined. There were such possibilities!</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The woman, she wouldn’t do. He’d known that from the start. But Doyle had two sisters, back in his great home awaiting his re- turn, and that was the trick. It was in the blood, in his blood, the priest had said so already. And if it could be in his blood it could be in their blood.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi pressed her fingertips against her forehead. The headache was growing stronger, and her vision blurred.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle. Sharp, he was. Always had been, and even when his body had failed him, his mind was a blade. Now the body was alive, vital, and he thrummed with eagerness.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The priest recognized his strength, whispered that he might be the future of their congregation, that a man like him was ne- cessary. The holy man was old and he feared for the future, for his little flock in the cave, for these timid folk. Picking through wreckage, scrambling in the dirt, that was their life. They’d fled here seeking safety and they’d survived till now, but the world was changing.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The holy man was right. Too right, perhaps. For Doyle indeed envisioned a deep change.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Lungs filled with water, the priest weighed down. What a sim- ple death!</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And then it was chaos and violence and smoke. Fire, fire, burn- ing. The cave was deemed almost a fortress by its inhabitants. When the tide came in, it was cut off from land and only ap- proachable by boat, rendering it a cozy, safe hideout. They hadn’t much, but they had this.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He was a single man and there were three dozen of them, but he’d killed the priest and now he held sway over them. He was holy. They were forced to remain on their knees as he set their bundles of cloth, their possessions, on fire. The cave filled with smoke.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">There was a boat. He pulled the woman into the boat. She obeyed, numb and afraid. As he rowed off, she stared at him, and he glanced away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He'd thought her unattractive. She was now frightfully ugly, with her belly grown and her eyes dull. But she was necessary. She would serve a purpose.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_183" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 183 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183126_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And then Noemi wasn’t with him as she’d been all this time, as close as his shadow. She was with someone else, a woman, with her fair hair falling loose around her shoulders as she spoke to another girl.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He has changed,” the young woman whispered. “Don’t you see it? His eyes are not the same.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The other girl, her hair plaited, shook her head.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi shook her head too. Their brother, gone on a long voyage and now returned and there were so many questions to ask, but he wouldn’t let them speak. And the first woman, she thought a horror had befallen him, that an evil possessed him, but the other one, she knew this had always been him, under the skin.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">I feared evil long ago. I feared him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Under the skin, and Noemi looked down at her hands, at her wrist, which itched terribly. Before she could scratch her- self pustules erupted and there rose tendrils, like hairs, upon her skin. Her velvety body fruited. Fleshy, white, fan-shaped caps sliced through her marrow and her muscle, and when she opened her mouth liquid poured up, gold and black, like a river that stained the floor.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A hand on her shoulder and a whisper in her ear.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Open your eyes,” said Noemi reflexively. Her mouth was full of blood and she spat out her own teeth.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_184" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 184 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183130_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">C C reathe. Just breathe,” he told her. He was a voice. She couldn't see him well, be- B cause the pain blurred her eyes and the tears didn’t help in that regard. He held her hair back as she vomited and helped her stand up. Black and gold specks danced under her eyelids when she closed them. She’d never felt this sick in her life.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’'ll die,” she croaked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You won't,” he assured her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Hadn't she died? She thought she had. There had been blood and bile in her mouth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She stared at the man. She thought she knew him, but his name escaped her. She was having trouble thinking, remem- bering, separating her thoughts from other thoughts. Other memories. Who was she?</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle, she’d been Doyle, and Doyle had killed all those people, burned them all.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The snake, it bites its tail.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The young, skinny man walked her out of the bathroom and pressed a glass of water against her lips.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She lay on the bed and turned her head. Francis sat on a chair, close to her, dabbing the sweat that beaded her forehead. Francis, yes. And she was Noemi Taboada, and this was High Place. It came back to her, the horror she’d been subjected to, the bloated body of Howard Doyle and his spit in her mouth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She recoiled. Francis froze, then slowly handed her the handkerchief he’d been holding. She clutched it in one hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What did you do to me?” she asked. It hurt to speak. Her throat felt scratchy. She recalled the filth that had poured into her mouth, and she suddenly wished to run into the bathroom again, to vomit her guts out.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Do you need to get up?” he asked, readying a hand to help her.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_185" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 185 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183135_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No,” she said, knowing she couldn’t reach the bathroom on her own, but also not wanting him to touch her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He slid his hands into his jacket’s pockets. The corduroy jacket she’d thought looked good on him. The bastard. She re- gretted every nice thing she’d ever thought of him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’m supposed to explain,” he said, his voice quiet.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“How the hell are you explaining that?! Howard...he... you...how?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Christ. She couldn't even put it into words. The damn hor- ror of it. Of the black bile in her mouth and then the vision she’d had.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’ll tell you the story and then you can ask me questions. I think that would be the easiest thing,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi didn’t want to do any talking. She didn’t think she could talk much, even if she tried. Better to let him speak, even if she felt like punching him. She was so tired, so sick.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I suppose now you realize we are not like other people and this house is not like other houses. A long time ago, Howard, he found a fungus which is able to extend human life quite a bit. It can cure diseases; it keeps you healthy.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T saw that. I saw him,” she muttered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You did?” Francis replied. “I suppose you entered the gloom. How deep have you gone into it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She stared at him. He was confusing her more. He shook his head.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The fungus, it runs under the house, all the way to the cemetery and back. It’s in the walls. Like a giant spider’s web. In that web we can preserve memories, thoughts, caught like the flies that wander into a real web. We call that repository of our thoughts, of our memories, the gloom.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“How is that possible?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Fungi can enter into symbiotic relationships with host plants. Mycorrhiza. Well, it turns out that it can also have a symbiotic relationship with humans. The mycorrhiza in this house creates the gloom.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You have access to ancestral memories because of a fun- gus.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. Only some of them are not full memories; you get faint echoes and they’re jumbled.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Like not being able to tune to a radio station, she thought. Noemi looked at the corner on the wall that was defaced by the black mold. “I’ve seen and dreamt very strange things. Are you telling me the house has done that? Because there’s a fungus running inside of it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_186" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 186 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183140_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Why would it do that to me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It wouldn't be intentional. I guess it’s in its nature.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Every damn vision she had experienced had been terrify- ing. Whatever this thing’s nature was, she couldn't begin to understand it. A nightmare. That’s what it was. A living night- mare, sins and malevolent secrets fastened together.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Then I was right about your house being haunted. And my cousin is not insane, she’s simply seen this gloom.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis nodded, and Noemi chuckled. No wonder Francis had been so agitated when she had suggested there was a ra- tional explanation to Catalina’s strange behavior and her talk of ghosts. Not that she would have guessed it was all con- nected to mushrooms.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She glanced at the oil lamp burning by her bedside and realized she had no idea how much time had passed. How long she’d been in the gloom. It could have been hours, it could have been days. She couldn't hear the patter of the rain anymore.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What did Howard Doyle do to me?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The fungus is in the walls of the house and it’s in the air. You don’t realize it, but you're breathing it in. Slowly, it has an effect on you. But if you come in contact with it in other ways, the effect can accelerate.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What did he do to me?” she repeated.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Most people who come in contact with this fungus die. That’s what happened to the workers in the mine. It killed them, some faster than others. But obviously not everyone perishes. Some people are more resistant to it. If they don’t die, though, it can still affect their mind.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Like Catalina?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Sometimes a little and sometimes worse than Catalina. It can burn out your own self. Our servants, you might have no- ticed they don’t talk much. There’s very little of them left. It’s almost like their mind has been carved out.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That’s not possible.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis shook his head. “Have you ever known an alco- holic? It affects their brain, and so does this.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are you telling me that’s what’s going to happen to Cata- lina? To me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No!” Francis said quickly. “No, no. They’re a special case, Great Uncle Howard calls them his bondservants, and the miners, they were mulch. But you can have a symbiotic rela- tionship with the fungus. None of that will happen to you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What will happen to me?”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_187" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 187 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183144_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis’s hands were still firmly in his pockets, but he was fidgeting. She could tell, the fingers clenching and unclench- ing. He was looking down at the cover on her bed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’ve told you about the gloom. I haven't told you about the bloodline. We’re special. The fungus bonds with us, it’s not noxious. It can even make us immortal. Howard has lived many lives, in many different bodies. He transfers his con- sciousness to the gloom and then from the gloom he can live again, in the body of one of his children.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He possesses his children?” Noemi said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No...he becomes...they become him...they become some- one new. Only the children, it goes down the bloodline. And for generations the bloodline has been kept isolated, to ensure we were all able to interact with the fungus, that we would keep this symbiotic relationship. No outsiders.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Incest. He married two women who were sisters, and he was going to marry Ruth to her cousin, and before that he must have...his sisters,” Noemi said, suddenly remembering the vision she’d had. The two young women. “He had two sis- ters. God, he had children with them.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The Doyle look. All the people in those portraits. “How far back?” Noemi asked. “How old is he? How many generations?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I don’t know. Three hundred years, maybe more.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Three hundred years. Marrying his own kin, having chil- dren with them, then transferring his mind into one of their bodies. Over and over again. And all of you? You allow this?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We have no choice. He’s a god.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You have a damn choice! And that sick fuck is not a god!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis stared at her. He had taken his hands out of his pockets and was now clutching them together. He looked tired. Slowly he slid a hand up and touched his forehead; he shook his head.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He is to us,” he said. “And he wants you to be part of our family.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Then that’s why he poured that black sludge down my throat.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“They were afraid you were going to leave. They couldn't let you do that. Now you won't be able to go anywhere.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I don’t want to be part of your god damn family, Francis,” she said. “And believe me, I’m going to go back home and I’m going to—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It won't let you go. My father, I don’t think I told you about him, did I?”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_188" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 188 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183149_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She had been looking at the black marks on the wall, the mold in the corner of her room, but she slowly turned her head to look at him. He had taken out a little portrait from his pocket. This is what he clutched in his hand, she thought. The little picture nestling in his jacket’s pocket.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Richard,” Francis whispered, allowing her to look at the black-and-white photograph of a man. “His name was Rich- ard.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The sharpness of Francis’s sallow face vaguely reminded her of Virgil Doyle, but now she could see the traces of his father: the pointed chin, the broad forehead.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Ruth caused a lot of damage. It wasn’t just the people she killed; she hurt Howard very badly. No normal man would have survived after she shot him, not the way she did it. He survived. But his grip, his power, decreased. That’s why we lost all our workers.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“They were all hypnotized? Like your three servants?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No. Not quite. He couldn't possibly manipulate that many people at once. It was a more subtle push and pull. It affected them, though. The house, the fungus, it affected the miners. It was a fog that could dull your senses when he needed it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What about your father?” she asked, handing him back the portrait, which he tucked in his pocket.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“After Howard was shot, he slowly began to heal himself. It has been hard, in recent generations, for the family to have children. When my mother came of age, Howard tried to...but he was too old, too damaged, to give her a child. And there were other troubles too.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">His niece. He tried to have a child with his niece, Noemi thought, and the fleeting idea of that hideous thing she’d seen naked laboring over a woman, pressing its emaciated body against Florence, made her want to hurl again. She pressed the handkerchief to her mouth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Noemi?” Francis asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What troubles?” she replied, urging him on.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Money. The remaining workers all left when Howard’s control over them snapped, and there was no one to watch the mine, so it flooded. There was no money coming in, and the Revolution had already ruined much of our finances. They needed money and they needed children. Otherwise, what would happen to the bloodline? My mother found my father, and she thought he’d do. He had a little money. Not a huge fortune, but enough to tide us over, and most important she thought he could get her with child. He came to live here, to</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_189" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 189 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183153_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">High Place. They had me. I was a boy, but the idea was that he might give her more children, that he might give her girls.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The gloom, it affected him. He felt himself going mad. He wanted to leave, but he couldn’t. He never could get far. He threw himself down a ravine in the end. If you fight it, then it will hurt. It will be bad,” Francis warned her. “But if you obey, if you bond with it, if you agree to be part of the family, then it will be fine.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina fights it, doesn’t she?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes,” Francis admitted. “But it’s also that she is not...she’s not quite as compatible—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi shook her head. “What makes you think I’ll comply any more than she has?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're compatible. Virgil, he picked Catalina because he knew she’d be compatible, but when you came here, it became obvious you're even more suitable than her. I guess they hope you'll be more understanding.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That I'll be happy to join your family. That I’ll be happy to what? Give you my money? Maybe give you children?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. Yes, to both.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're a pack of monsters. And you! I trusted you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He stared at her intently, his mouth quivering. She thought he might cry. It made her furious. That he should be the one breaking down and weeping. Don’t you dare, she thought.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tm so sorry.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Sorry! You god damn bastard!” she yelled, and despite the fact that her body still throbbed with a horrible, dull pain she stood up.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I am sorry. I didn’t want this,” he said, pushing his chair back, getting up too.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Then help me! Get me out of here!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T can't.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She hit him. It wasn’t a good punch, and as soon as she threw it she thought she was going to collapse on the floor. It robbed her of all strength, and she felt suddenly boneless. If he hadn't caught her, surely she’d have cracked her head open. Yet she scrambled against him, trying to shove him off.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Let go,” she demanded, but her voice was muffled against the folds of his jacket. She couldn’t pull her head up.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You need to rest. ’'ll think of a solution, but you need to rest,” he whispered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Go to hell!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He deposited her carefully on the bed again, pulling the covers up, and she wanted to tell him to go to hell one last time, but her eyes were closing, and in the corner of the room</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_190" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 190 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183158_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">the mold was beating like a heart, stretching out, making the wallpaper ripple. The floorboards also swayed, trembling like the skin of a living thing.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A great snake rose from under the floorboards, slick and black, and slid over the covers. Noemi stared at it as it touched her legs, its skin cool against her feverish flesh, and she didn’t move, fearing it would rear its head back and bite her. And upon the snake’s skin there were a thousand tiny little growths, tiny pulsating points, which quivered and unleashed spores.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It’s another dream, she thought. It’s the gloom, and the gloom isn’t real.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But she didn’t want to see this, she didn’t want to, and she moved her legs at last, trying to kick the thing away. When she touched the snake its skin split open, and it was white and dead, the carcass of a snake ravaged by decay. Life teemed upon this white corpse, mold blooming all over it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Et Verbum caro factum est, said the snake.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was on her knees now. The chamber was cold and made of stone. It was dark; there were no windows. They’d set candles upon an altar, but it was still much too dark. The altar was more elaborate than the one she'd spied in the caves. The table was covered with a red velvet cloth and silver candela- bra. But it was still dark and humid and cold.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard Doyle had added tapestries too. Red and black, the ouroboros displayed on them. Pageantry, Doyle understood pageantry was an important part of this game. There he was, Doyle, clad in crimson. Next to him stood the woman from the caves, heavily pregnant, and looking ill.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Et Verbum caro factum est, the snake told her, whispering secrets in her ear. The snake was gone, but she could still hear it. It had a peculiar, hoarse voice, and Noemi had no idea what it was telling her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Two women were helping her down a dais, to lie down at the foot of the altar. Two blond women. She’d seen them too, before. The sisters. And she’d seen this ritual before. In the cemetery. The woman giving birth in the cemetery.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Birth. The child cried out, and Doyle held the child. And then she knew.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Et Verbum caro factum est.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She knew what she had not properly seen in her previous dreams, and she did not wish to see now, but there it was. The knife and the child. Noemi closed her eyes, but even behind her eyelids she saw it all, crimson and black and the child torn apart and they were eating him.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_191" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 191 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183202_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Flesh of the gods.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They held their hands up, and Doyle deposited bits of flesh, bits of bone, into their hands and they chewed this pale meat.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They’d done this before, in the caves. But it had been the priests, when they died, who offered their flesh. Doyle had per- fected the ritual. Clever Doyle, who was well learned, and had read plenty of books on theology, biology, medicine, looking for answers, and now he’d found them.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi’s eyes were still closed, and the woman’s eyes were closed too. They pressed a cloth against her face, and Noemi thought they would kill the woman now, they would also cut up her body and ingest it. But she was wrong on this point. They swaddled the body. Swaddled it tight, and there was a pit by the altar, and they were throwing her into it but she was alive.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She’s not dead, Noemi told them. But it didn’t matter. It was a memory.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was necessary, always is. The fungus would erupt up, from her body, up through the soil, weaving itself into the walls, ex- tending itself into the foundations of the building. And the gloom needed a mind. It needed her. The gloom was alive. It was alive in more than one way; at its rotten core there was the corpse of a woman, her limbs twisted, her hair brittle against the skull. And the corpse stretched its jaws open, screaming inside the earth, and from her dried lips emerged the pale mushroom.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The priest would have sacrificed himself: part of the body de- voured, the rest buried. Life erupting from those remains, and the congregation tied to him. Tied ultimately to their god. But Doyle was no fool who would offer himself in sacrifice.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle could be a god without having to obey their arcane, fool- ish rules.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle was a god.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle existed, persisted.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle always is.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Monsters. Monstrum, ah, is that what you think of me, Noemi?</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Have you seen enough, curious girl?” Doyle asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He was playing cards in a corner of her room. She watched his wrinkled hands, the amber ring upon his index finger flashing bright under the light of the candles as he shuffled the cards. He raised his head to look at her. She stared at him. It was the Doyle of now. Howard Doyle, his spine bent, his breath labored. He placed three cards down, carefully turning each one. A knight with a sword and a page holding a coin. She</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_192" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 192 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183207_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">could see, through the thin shirt he wore, the black boils dot- ting his back.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Why do you show me this?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The house shows you. The house loves you. Are you enjoy- ing our hospitality? Would you like to play with me?” he asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“A pity,” he said, revealing the third card: a single, empty cup. “You'll still renounce yourself in the end. You're already like us, you’re family. You don’t know it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You don’t scare me, you piece of shit monster, with your dreams and your tricks. This isn’t real, and you'll never keep me here.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You really think that?” he asked, and the boils rippled down his back. A trickle of black liquid, as black as ink, dripped onto the floor beneath him. “I can make you do anything I want.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He sliced one of the pustules in his hands with a long nail, pressing it against a silver cup—it looked like the goblet in the card he’d been holding—and it broke, filling the cup with a foul liquid. “Have a drink,” he said, and for a second she felt compelled to step forward and take a sip, before revulsion and alarm froze her limbs.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He smiled. He was trying to show her his power; even in dreams he was the master.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tl kill you when I wake up. Give me a chance, I'll kill you,” she swore.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She threw herself at him, sinking her fingers into his flesh, wringing the thin neck. It was like parchment, it tore beneath her hands, muscle and blood vessels showing. He grinned at her, with Virgil’s brutish smile. He was Virgil. She squeezed harder, and then he was pushing her back, his thumb pressing against her lips, against her teeth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis looked at her, his eyes wide with pain, his hand sliding down. She let go of him and stepped back. Francis opened his mouth, to plead with her, and a hundred maggots crawled out of his maw.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Worms, stems, the snake in the grass rose and wrapped it- self around Noemi’s neck.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">You’re ours, like it or not. You’re ours and you’re us.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She tried to peel the snake off her, but it knotted itself tight, digging into her flesh, and it opened its jaws, ready to devour her whole. Noemi dug her nails into the snake, and it whis- pered “Et Verbum caro factum est.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But a woman’s voice also spoke, and she said, “Open your eyes.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_193" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 193 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183211_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">I must remember that, she thought. I must remember to open my eyes. 54 mins left in book 68%</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_194" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 194 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183216_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">aylight. She’d never been more thankful for such</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">an ordinary sight, the beams of light filtering</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">under the curtains making her heart soar. Noemi</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">flung the curtain aside and pressed her palms against the window. She tried the door. It was, predictably, locked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They had left a tray with food for her. The tea had gone cold, and she didn’t dare drink it, wondering what might be in it. Even the toast gave her pause. She ended up nibbling at the edges of the slice of bread and drinking water from the bath- room’s faucet.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">If the fungus was in the air, though, did it matter? She was inhaling it anyway. The closet door was open, and she could see they’d emptied her suitcase and returned all her dresses to their hangers.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was cold, so she put on her long-sleeved plaid dress with the Peter Pan collar and the matching white cuffs. It was warm enough, even if she had never quite favored plaid. She couldn’t even remember why she’d packed it, but she was glad she had.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Once she’d combed her hair and put on her shoes, Noemi tried to open the window again, but it didn’t budge. Neither did the door. The cutlery they’d left included a spoon, which would be of no help to her. Just as she was wondering if the spoon could be used to pry the door open, the key turned inthe lock, and Florence stood at the doorway. As usual, she seemed extremely peeved to see Noemi. The feeling, that day, was en- tirely mutual.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Do you intend to starve yourself?” Florence asked, eyeing the tray by the door, which Noemi had barely touched.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Can't say I have much of an appetite after what happened,” Noemi replied flatly.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You'll have to eat. In any case, Virgil wants to see you. He’s waiting in the library. Come along.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_195" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 195 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183220_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi followed the woman down the hallways and down the stairs. Florence did not speak to her, and Noemi moved two steps behind her at all times, until they reached the ground floor and Noemi dashed toward the front door. She feared they might have locked it, but the door handle turned, and she burst out into the misty morning. It was quite thick, this mist, but it didn’t matter. She dashed blindly into it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Tall grasses brushed her body, and her dress caught on something. She heard it ripping, but she tugged at the skirt and kept going. It was raining, the slightest drizzle dampening her hair. And even if there had been thunder and lightning and hail she wouldn't have stopped.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But Noemi did in fact stop. She was suddenly out of breath, and even when she stood still, tried to calm herself and breathe in, she could hardly accomplish it. She felt as though a hand were squeezing her throat and she gasped, stumbling against a tree, its low branches scratching her temple, and let out a sharp hiss, touching her head, feeling blood under her fingertips.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She needed to walk more slowly, needed to see where she was going, but the mist was thick, and the breathlessness did not subside. She slipped, tumbling to the ground, and lost a shoe. It was there and suddenly gone.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi attempted to push herself up to her feet again, but the relentless pressure against her throat made it difficult to summon the necessary strength. She managed to get on her knees. Blindly she tried to reach for the missing shoe and gave up on it. It didn’t matter where it was. She tugged the other shoe off.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Barefoot, she’d continue on barefoot. She clutched her re- maining shoe in one hand, trying to think. The mist shrouded everything. The trees and the shrubs and the house. She had no idea in which direction she should go, but she could hear the grasses rustling, and she was certain someone was coming for her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She still couldn’t breathe, her throat was on fire. She gasped, trying to force air into her lungs. Noemi dug her fin- gers into the wet earth and stood up, dragging herself forward. Four, five, six steps before she stumbled again and was back on her knees.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was too late by then. Through the mist came a tall, dark figure, which bent down next to her. She raised her hands, to ward it off, to no avail. He bent down, the man, he picked her up as easily as one lifted a rag doll, and she shook her head.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_196" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 196 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183225_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She struck, blindly, the shoe hitting his face, and he let out an angry grunt. He released her, dropping her in the mud. Noemi shifted forward, ready to crawl away if it came to it, but she hadn’t really hurt him, and he clutched her, pulled her into his arms.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He was taking her back to the house, and she couldn’t even protest; it was as if in the struggle her throat had been sealed almost completely shut and now she could barely draw in any air. To make it even worse, she realized how close the house really was, how she’d scarcely walked more than a few meters before collapsing on the ground.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She saw the porch, the front entrance, and turned her head to look up at the man.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil. He’d opened the door and now they were going up the stairs. The round, colored glass window at the top of the staircase had a thin snake etched in red around the rim. She hadn't noticed it before, but now the image was clear: the snake was biting its tail.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They headed to her room and into the bathroom. He gin- gerly placed her in the bathtub, and she gasped as he opened the tap and water began to flow into the tub.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Get out of those clothes and clean yourself,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The shortness of breath was gone. Like flipping a switch. But her heart was still racing, and she stared at him, her mouth slightly open, her hands holding on to the sides of the bathtub.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You'll catch a cold,” he said simply, and he stretched out a hand, as if to undo the top button of her dress.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi slapped his hand away and clutched the collar of her dress. “Don’t!” she yelled, and it hurt to speak, that one word slicing her tongue.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He chuckled, amused. “This is your fault, Noemi. You de- cided to take a tumble in the mud, in the rain, and now you must wash yourself. So, get out of those clothes before I make you,” he said. There was no threat in his voice; he sounded very measured, but his face was infused with a simmering animosity.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She undid the buttons with shaky hands and took the dress off, crumpling it into a ball and tossing it on the floor. She was left in her underthings. She thought that humiliation would be enough for him, but he leaned against the wall and cocked his head, looking at her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Well?” he said. “You're filthy. Take off everything and wash yourself. Your hair is a mess.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“As soon as you step out of the bathroom.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_197" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 197 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183230_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He grabbed the three-legged stool and sat down, looking unperturbed. “I’m not going anywhere.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m not getting naked in front of you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He leaned forward, as if to share a secret with her. “I can make you get out of those clothes. It won’t take me a minute, and I will hurt you. Or you can take them off yourself, like a good girl.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He meant it. She still felt light-headed, and the water was too hot, but she peeled off her undergarments and tossed them away, to rest in a corner of the bathroom. She grabbed the bar of soap sitting on a porcelain dish and scrubbed her head, soaped her arms and her hands. She worked quickly, rinsing the soap out.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil had closed the tap, his left elbow resting on the edge of the tub. At least he was looking at the floor rather than at her, apparently content to admire the tiles. He rubbed his mouth with his fingers.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You cut my lip with your shoe,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">There was a trail of blood on his lips, and Noemi was glad that at least she’d managed that. “Is that why you're torturing me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Torture? I wanted to make sure you didn’t faint in the bath. It would be a pity if you drowned while in the tub.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You could have stood guard outside the door, you pig,” she told him, brushing a wet strand of her hair away from her face.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. But that wouldn’t be half as much fun,” he replied. His grin would have been charming if she’d met him at a party, if she didn’t know him. He had fooled Catalina with that smile, but it was a predator’s grin. It made her want to hit him again, to beat him in the name of her cousin.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The faucet was dripping. Plop, plop, plop. It was the only noise in the bathroom. She raised a hand, pointed behind him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You can pass me the robe now.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He didn’t reply.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I said, you can—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">His hand dipped into the water, settling on her leg, and Noemi pushed herself back, slamming against the tub, mak- ing water splash onto the floor. Her instinct was to stand up, jump out of the tub, and run out of the room. But the position he occupied meant her path would be blocked if she did. He knew it too. The tub, the water, seemed to the young woman her shield, and she drew her knees against her chest.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Get out,” she said, trying to sound firm rather than afraid.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What? Are you suddenly bashful?” he asked. “Last time we were here it wasn’t the case.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_198" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 198 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183234_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That was a dream,” she stammered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It doesn’t mean it wasn’t real.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She blinked incredulously at him, and she opened her mouth to protest. Virgil leaned forward, his hand settling on the back of her neck, and she shrieked, pushing him away, but he’d gotten hold of her hair and was tipping her head back, pulling it hard.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He’d done that in the dream, or a similar motion. Pulled her head up and kissed her, and afterward she’d wanted him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She tried to turn her head away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Virgil,” Francis said loudly. He was standing by the door- way, his hands curled into tight fists at his side.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil turned his head toward his cousin. “Yes?” he said, his voice hard.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Dr. Cummins is here. He’s ready to see her.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil let out a sigh and gave Noemi a shrug, releasing her. “Well, it seems we'll continue our chat some other time,” he declared and walked out of the bathroom.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She had not expected him to release her, and her relief was so profound she pressed both hands against her mouth and bent forward, gasping.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Dr. Cummins wants to check up on you. Do you need help getting out of the tub?” Francis asked. He spoke softly.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She shook her head. Her face was burning, flushed with mortification.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis had grabbed a folded towel from a pile upon a shelf, and he wordlessly handed it to her. She looked up at him and clutched the towel.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tll be in the room,” Francis said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He walked out of the bathroom and closed the door behind him. Noemi dried herself and put on her robe.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">When she stepped out of the bathroom Dr. Cummins was standing by the bed and gestured for her to sit down on it. He took Noemi’s pulse, checked her heartbeat, then opened a bot- tle with rubbing alcohol and dampened a ball of cotton with it. He pressed the piece of cotton against her temple. Noemi had forgotten about the scratch she’d incurred, and she winced.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“How is she?” Francis asked. He was standing behind the doctor, looking anxious.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“She’ll be fine. There’s nothing but a couple of scrapes. It won't even necessitate a bandage. But it shouldn’t have hap- pened. I thought you had explained to her the situation al- ready,” the doctor said. “If she’d damaged her face Howard would have been very sore about it.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_199" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 199 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183239_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You shouldn't be mad at him. Francis did explain that I’m in a house full of incestuous monsters and their toadies,” Noemi replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Dr. Cummins stilled his fingers and frowned. “Well. You haven’t lost that charming way of addressing your elders. Filla glass with water, Francis,” the doctor said as he continued dab- bing at her hairline. “The girl is dehydrated.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I can manage,” she replied, snatching away the piece of cotton and pressing it against her head.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The doctor shrugged and tossed his stethoscope in his black bag. “Francis was supposed to talk to you, but he must not have made himself clear last night. You can’t leave this house, Miss Taboada. No one can. It won’t let you. If you try to run off, you'll suffer another attack like the one you had.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“How can a house do that?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It can. That is all that matters.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis approached the bed with the glass of water and handed it to her. Noemi took a couple of sips, carefully eyeing both men. Cummins’s face caught her eye; there was a detail she had not noticed before and which now seemed obvious.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're related to them, aren’t you? You're another Doyle.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Distantly, which is why I live in the village, managing the family’s affairs,” the doctor replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Distantly. That sounded like a joke. She didn’t think there was any distance in the Doyle family tree. It didn’t branch at all. Virgil had said he’d married Dr. Cummins’s daughter, which meant that, to boot, they’d attempted to pull that “dis- tant” relation back into their bosom.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He wants you to be part of our family, Francis had said. Noemi clutched the glass with both hands.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You must have your breakfast. Francis, bring the tray here,” the doctor commanded.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’ve lost my appetite.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t be silly. Francis, the tray.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Is the tea warm? I’ll very much enjoy tossing a scalding cup in the good doctor’s face,” she said lightly.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The doctor took off his glasses and began cleaning them with a handkerchief, his brow furrowed. “It seems you are determined to be difficult today. I shouldn’t be surprised. Women can be terribly mercurial.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Was your daughter difficult?” Noemi asked. The doctor raised his head sharply and stared at her, and she knew she’d struck a nerve. “You gave them your own daughter.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I have no idea what you are talking about,” he muttered.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_200" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 200 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183243_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Virgil said she ran away, but it’s not true. No one leaves this place, you said so. It would never have let her go. She’s dead, isn’t she? Did he kill her?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi and the doctor stared at each other. The doctor stood up stiffly, snatching the glass from her hands and set- ting it on the night table.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Perhaps if you'd let us speak, the two of us alone,” Francis told the older man.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Dr. Cummins clasped Francis by the arm and gave Noemi a narrow look. “Yes. You must talk sense into her. He won’t toler- ate this behavior, you know it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Before exiting the room, the doctor paused at the foot of the bed, his medical bag held tight in one hand, and addressed Noemi. “My daughter died in childbirth, if you must know. She couldn't give the family the child they needed. Howard thinks you and Catalina will be hardier. Different blood. We'll see.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He closed the door behind him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis grabbed the silver tray and brought it to the bed. Noemi clutched the covers. “You really must eat,” he told her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Isn't it poisoned?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He leaned down, set the tray upon her lap, whispering in Spanish to her ear. “The food you've had, the tea, they’ve been laced with something, yes. But the egg is fine, start eating. I'll tell you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“In Spanish,” he said. “He can hear, through the walls, through the house, but he doesn’t speak Spanish. He won't understand. Keep your voice low and eat, I’m serious. You are dehydrated and you vomited so much last night.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi stared at him. Slowly she grabbed a spoon and tapped the hard-boiled egg’s shell without taking her eyes off him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I want to help you,” he said, “but it’s difficult. You’ve seen what the house can do.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Keep you inside, apparently. Is it true I can’t leave?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It can induce you to do certain things and stop you from doing others.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Control your mind.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“In a way. It’s more rudimentary than that. There’s certain instincts it triggers.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I couldn't breathe.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I know.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Slowly Noemi nibbled at a bit of egg. When she was done he pointed at the toast, nodding, but shook his head at the jam.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There must be a way to get out of here.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_201" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 201 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183248_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There might be.” He took out a little flask from his pocket and showed it to her. “Recognize this?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s the medicine I gave my cousin. What are you doing with it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Dr. Cummins told me to get rid of it after that episode, but I didn’t. The fungus, it’s in the air, and my mother makes sure it’s in your food. That’s how, slowly, it gets a hold of you. But it’s very sensitive to certain triggers. It doesn’t really like light much, nor certain scents.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“My cigarettes,” she said, snapping her fingers. “It irritates the house. And this tincture, it must irritate it too.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Did the healer in town know this? Or had it been a happy accident? Catalina had figured out the tincture had an effect on the house, that was certain. Accidental or intentional, her cousin had discovered the key even if she had been prevented from turning it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It does more than that,” Francis said. “It interferes with it. You take this tincture, the house, the fungus, will loosen its hold on you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“How can you be sure about that?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina. She tried to run away, but Virgil and Arthur caught her and brought her back. They found the draught she’d been taking and determined it was affecting the house’s control on her, so they took it away. But they didn’t realize this had been going on for a little while, and she must have asked someone in town to post a letter for her.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina, clever girl. She’d devised a fail-safe mechanism and had summoned help. Unfortunately, now Noemi, the would-be rescuer, was also trapped.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She reached for the flask, but he caught her hand and shook his head. “Remember what happened to your cousin? Take too much at once and you'll have a seizure.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Then it’s useless.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Far from it. You'll have to drink a little bit each time. Look, Dr. Cummins is here for a reason. Great Uncle Howard is going to die. There’s no stopping it. The fungus extends your life, but it can’t keep you going forever. His body will give way soon, and afterward he’ll begin the transmigration. He will take pos- session of Virgil’s body. When that happens, when he dies, everyone will be distracted. They’ll be busy clustering around both of them. And the house will be weakened.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“When will this happen?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It can’t be too long,” Francis said. “You’ve seen Howard.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi didn’t really want to remember what she’d seen. She put down the bit of egg she had been nibbling and frowned.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_202" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 202 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183252_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He wants you to be part of the family. Go along with it, be patient, and I’ll get you out of here. There are tunnels, they lead to the cemetery, and I think I can hide supplies in them.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What does ‘go along with it’ mean exactly?” Noemi asked, because Francis was evading her eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She caught his chin with one hand, made him look at her. He stood perfectly still, holding his breath.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He’d like you to marry me. He’d like you to have children with me. He wants you to be one of us,” Francis said at last.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“And if I say no? What then?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He'll have his way.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He'll carve my mind out, like the servants? Or simply rape me?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tt won't come to that,” Francis muttered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Why?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Because he enjoys controlling people in other ways. It would be too coarse. He let my father go to town for years, he let Catalina go to church. He even let Virgil and my mother get far away from town and find spouses. He knows he needs people to obey his will and do his bidding, and they must wel- come it, otherwise it’s too exhausting.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“And he can’t control them all the time,” Noemi ventured. “Ruth was able to grab a rifle, after all, and Catalina tried to tell me the truth.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That’s right. And Catalina wouldn't reveal who'd given her the tonic, no matter how much Howard tried to wrestle that information from her.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Plus the miners had organized a strike. As much as Howard Doyle would like to believe himself a god, he couldn’t push and force everyone to submit to him every hour of the day. And yet, in decades past, he must have been able to subtly manipu- late a great number of people, and when that wasn’t enough he could kill them or make them disappear, like with Benito.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Outright confrontation won’t work,” Francis said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi examined the butter knife and knew he was right. What could she do? Kick and punch and she’d end up right where she was, perhaps even worse off. “If I agree to go along with this charade, then you must get Catalina out too.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis did not reply, but she could guess that he wasn’t enjoying the idea of springing two people out by the way he frowned.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I can’t leave her behind,” she said, clutching the hand in which he still held the bottle. “You must also give her the tinc- ture, you must also break her free.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes, fine. Keep your voice down.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_203" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 203 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183257_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She let go of his hand and lowered her voice. “You must promise, on your life.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m promising. Now, shall we give it a try?” he asked, tak- ing out the bottle’s glass stopper. “It’ll make you a little sleepy, but you probably need the rest.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Virgil can see my dreams,” she muttered, pressing her knuckles against her mouth for a moment. “Won't he know, if he can see my dreams? Won’t he know what I’m thinking?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“They’re not really dreams. It’s the gloom. But be careful when you're there.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I don’t know if I can trust you,” she said. “Why would you help me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He was unlike his cousin in a thousand tiny ways, with his slim hands and his weak mouth, spindly where Virgil was solid forcefulness. He was young and wan, and infected with kindness. But who could say if it was all for show, if he couldn’t sink into ruthless indifference. After all, nothing in this place was what it seemed. There were secrets upon secrets.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She touched the back of her neck, the place where Virgil’s fingers had dug into her hair.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis twirled the glass stopper in one hand. It caught a stray ray of light, filtering through the curtains; a tiny prism, painting a rainbow on the edge of her bed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There’s a cicada fungus. Massospora cicadina. I remember reading a journal article which discussed its appearance: the fungus sprouts along the abdomen of the cicada. It turns it into a mass of yellow powder. The journal said the cicadas, which had been so grossly infected, were still ‘singing,’ as their body was consumed from within. Singing, calling for a mate, half dead. Can you imagine?” Francis said. “You're right, I do have a choice. I’m not going to end my life singing a tune, pre- tending everything is fine.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He ceased toying with the glass stopper and glanced at her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You managed to pretend so far.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She stared at him, and he stared back at her gravely. “Yes,” he said. “And now you're here and I can’t anymore.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She watched him, silent, as he poured out a minute amount of liquid onto a spoon. Noemi swallowed the tincture. It was bitter. He offered her the napkin that had been set by her plate, and Noemi wiped her mouth clean.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Let me take this away,” Francis said, placing the bottle back in his pocket and picking up her tray. She touched his arm, and he stopped.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Thanks.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_204" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 204 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183302_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t thank me,” he replied. “I should have spoken sooner, but I’m a coward.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She pressed her head back against the pillows after that and let the drowsiness take over. Later—she wasn’t sure how much later—she heard a rustling of cloth and sat up. Ruth Doyle was perched at the foot of her bed, looking down at the floor.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Not Ruth. A memory? A ghost? Not quite a ghost. She realized that what she had been seeing, the voice whispering to her, urging her to open her eyes, was the mind of Ruth, which still nestled in the gloom, in the crevices and mold- covered walls. There must be other minds, bits of persons, hid- den underneath the wallpaper, but none as solid, as tangible as Ruth.
