      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She looked at Virgil still sprawled on the floor and con- sidered sticking the knife into his skull, but her hands were trembling again, and she needed to get out of there and away from him. She must fetch Catalina. There was no time to waste.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_230" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 230 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183500_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">oemi rushed along the darkened hallway, a hand</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">on the wall to steady herself. The lights that</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">were working seemed spectral and awfully dim,</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">flickering in and out of life, but she knew the path by memory.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Quickly, quickly, she told herself.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi feared her cousin’s room would be locked, but she turned the doorknob and yanked the door open.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina sat on the bed in a white nightgown. She was not alone. Mary kept her company, her eyes fixed on the floor.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina, we’re leaving,” Noemi said, extending a hand in her cousin’s direction while she held the razor in the other.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina did not move; she did not even acknowledge Noemi, her gaze lost.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina,” she repeated. The young woman didn’t budge.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi bit her lip and walked in, her eyes fixed on the maid sitting in the corner, her hand trembling as she gripped the razor. “For God’s sake, Catalina, snap out of it,” she said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But it was the maid who raised her head, golden eyes zero- ing in on Noemi, and rushed toward her, shoving her against the vanity. Her hands wrapped around Noemi’s throat. It was such a startling attack, the strength of the woman unthink- able for someone her age, that Noemi dropped the blade. Sev- eral items on the vanity also clattered and fell: perfume bottles and a hair comb and a picture of Catalina in a silver frame.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The maid pushed harder, forcing Noemi to step back, the hands at her throat squeezing tight and wood digging against her back. She tried to grasp something, anything as a weapon, but her fingers found nothing suitable, tugging at a doily, overturning a porcelain pitcher that rolled upon the ground and cracked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Ours,” the maid said. It didn’t sound like the woman’s voice. It was an odd, raspy sound. It was the voice of the house,</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_231" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 231 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183505_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">the voice of someone or something else, reproduced and ap- proximated by these vocal cords.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi attempted to pry the fingers off her neck, but those hands were more like claws, and all Noemi managed was to gasp and tug at the womar’s hair, which accomplished noth- ing.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Ours,” Mary repeated, then clenched her teeth like a wild animal, and Noemi could hardly see, so bad was the pain. Her eyes watered, her throat was on fire.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Suddenly the woman was yanked away and Noemi was able to breathe, taking in air in huge, desperate gulps as she gripped the dresser with one hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis had walked in, and he had pulled the maid off Noemi, but now the woman was clawing at him, her mouth opening wide to unleash a hideous screech. She shoved him down, onto the floor, her hands around his neck, bent over him like a bird of prey ready to devour a piece of carrion.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi picked up the straight razor and approached them. “Stop!” she cried out, and the woman turned around, screech- ing at Noemi, ready to sink those hands into her neck again and crush her windpipe.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She knew then a dizzy sort of terror, pure and overwhelm- ing, and she slashed at the woman’s throat. Once, twice, three times, blade meeting flesh, and the woman did not cry out. She fell to the ground in silence, face-first.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Blood dripped down Noemi’s fingers, and Francis raised his head and looked up at her, dazed. He stood up and stepped to- ward her. “Are you hurt?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She rubbed her neck with her free hand and stared at the dead woman on the floor. She must be dead. Noemi dare not turn the body over to look at her face, but there was a pool of blood growing underneath her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Her heart beat with a terrible, thundering force, and the blood dripped down, dirtying the pretty antique dress, dirty- ing her fingers. She slid the blade into her pocket, rubbed the tears from her eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Noemi?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He was now in front of her, blocking her view, and she snapped her eyes up to stare into his wan face. “Where were you?” she asked, fingers furiously clutching the lapels of his frock coat, and she wanted to beat him for not being with her, for leaving her alone.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Locked in my room,” he said. “I had to break out. I had to find you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You aren't lying? You didn’t abandon me?”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_232" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 232 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183511_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No! Please, are you hurt?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She chuckled. A ghastly chuckle, since she had fended off a rapist and escaped being choked to death.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Noemi,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He sounded worried. He should be. They should all be ter- ribly worried. She let go of him. “We have to get out of here.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She turned to Catalina. Her cousin was still sitting on the bed. She had not moved, except to press a hand against her open mouth. Her eyes were fixed on the lifeless body of the maid. Noemi pulled the covers away and grabbed her cousin’s hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Come on,” she said, and when Catalina wouldn’t move, she turned to Francis, whose suit was now smeared with her bloody fingerprints. “What’s wrong with her?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“They must have drugged her again. Without the tincture</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi took her cousin’s face between her hands and spoke firmly. “We're leaving.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina did not react. She wasn’t looking at Noemi. Her eyes were glassy. Noemi saw a pair of slippers by the bed and grabbed them, fitting them on Catalina’s feet. Then Noemi yanked Catalina by the arm, pulling her out of the bed. Cata- lina followed her, docile.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They hurried down the hallway. In her white nightgown Catalina looked like a second bride. Two ghost brides, Noemi thought.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Ahead of them a shadow emerged from within a pool of darkness, darting onto their path and startling Noemi.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Stop,” Florence said. Her face was very composed. Her voice did not sound anxious. She carried a gun in her hand ra- ther casually, as if this were a regular occurrence.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They stood still. Noemi had the razor, but even as she tight- ened her grip around its wood handle she knew she didn’t stand much of a chance, and Florence was aiming squarely at her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Drop that,” Florence said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi’s hand trembled, and the blood made the handle slick, difficult to hold, but she held it up. At her side, Catalina was trembling too.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You can’t make me.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Drop it, I said,” Florence repeated.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Her preternaturally calm voice had not wavered, but in her cold eyes Noemi could read savage murder, yet Noemi did not let go of the weapon until the woman shifted her aim, point-</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_233" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 233 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183516_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">ing at Catalina. The threat was clear, there was no need to speak it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi swallowed and dropped the weapon.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Turn around and start walking,” Florence commanded.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They did. Back the way they’d come, until they reached Howard’s room with the fireplace and the twin paintings of his wives. The old man lay in the ornate bed, as before, and Dr. Cummins sat at his side. The doctor’s bag was open, resting upon a side table, and now he took out a scalpel from it and pricked a couple of boils on Howard’s lips and cut through a thin film that seemed to cover his mouth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">This must have eased the man’s pain, for Howard sighed. Dr. Cummins placed the scalpel next to the bag and wiped his forehead with the back of his hand and let out a grunt.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There you are,” he said, rounding the bed. “It has acceler- ated. He can’t breathe properly. We need to begin.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s her,” Florence said, “and the trouble she has caused. Mary is dead.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard lay propped against a considerable number of pil- lows. His mouth was open, and he was making a wheezing sound as he clutched at the covers with his gnarled hands. His skin seemed the color of wax, the veins very dark, standing out against such paleness, a trickle of black bile falling down his chin.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Dr. Cummins raised a hand, pointing a finger at Francis. “You get over here,” he told the young man. “Where's Virgil?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Hurt. I felt his pain earlier,” Florence said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There’s no time to fetch him. The transmigration must take place now,” the doctor muttered, sinking his hands into a small basin with water and washing them clean. “Francis is here, and that’s what matters.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You can’t mean him,” Noemi said, shaking her head. “It’s not supposed to be him.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Of course it’s him,” Florence said. Her countenance was cool and collected.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Suddenly Noemi understood. Why would Howard have forfeited his son, his favorite? It made sense that he would pick the boy he cared little about, whose mind he might ob- literate without remorse. Had this, then, been their game all along? To slide Howard into Francis’s skin in the middle of the night and then for him to slide into Noemi’s bed? An impostor. But she wouldn’t have known at once, and maybe they figured afterward it wouldn’t matter. That she would be content, hav- ing taken a liking to Francis’s shell.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“But you can’t,” Noemi mumbled.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_234" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 234 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183520_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis was walking meekly toward the doctor. Noemi tried to grab his arm, but Florence intercepted her and pulled her toward a black velvet chair, forcing her to sit down. Cata- lina trailed around the room looking lost, standing at the foot of the bed, before walking a bit more and settling at the head of it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It could have all been easy and quiet,” Florence said, star- ing at Noemi. “You could be sitting calmly in your room, but you had to cause a ruckus.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Virgil tried to rape me,” Noemi said. “He tried to rape me, and I should have killed him back there.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Shush,” Florence replied, looking disgusted. Things were never spoken at High Place, not even now.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi made a motion as if to rise, but Florence pointed the gun at her. She sat back again, gripping the chair’s arms. Francis had now reached Howard’s bedside and was speaking to the doctor, their voices low.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He’s your son,” Noemi whispered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s a body,” Florence replied, her face stiff.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A body. That’s what they all were to them. The bodies of miners in the cemetery, the bodies of women who gave birth to their children, and the bodies of those children who were simply the fresh skin of the snake. And there on the bed lay the body that mattered. The father.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Dr. Cummins placed a hand on Francis’s shoulder, pushing him down. Francis fell to his knees and clutched his hands to- gether, penitent.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Bow your head, we will pray,” Florence ordered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi did not obey at once, but then Florence smacked her head, hard. The woman’s hand felt well practiced. The sting of the blow made black dots dance before Noemi’s eyes. She won- dered if they had also delivered such blows upon Ruth, teach- ing her obedience.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi clutched her hands together.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">On the other side of the bed, Catalina, mute still, imitated them, also clasping her hands. Her cousin did not look dis- tressed. Her face was immutable.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Et Verbum caro factum est,” Howard said, his voice thick and low, his amber ring flashing as he raised a hand in the air.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard recited a series of words that Noemi could not understand, yet she realized that understanding was not ne- cessary. Obedience, acceptance, that was what he required. For the old man, there was pleasure in witnessing this submis- sion.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_235" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 235 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183525_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Renounce yourself, that’s what he had demanded in the dream. That’s what mattered now. There was a physical com- ponent to this process, but there was also a mental one. A sur- render that must be granted. Perhaps there was even pleasure in such submission.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Renounce yourself.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi looked up. Francis was whispering, his lips mov- ing softly. Dr. Cummins and Florence and Howard were also whispering, all of them speaking in unison. This low whisper sounded, oddly, like a single voice. As if all their voices had coalesced into one mouth and it was that mouth that spoke, growing louder, rising like the tide.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The buzzing that Noemi had heard before began now, also growing louder. It sounded like hundreds of bees were hiding beneath the floorboards and the walls.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard had raised his hands, as if to cup the young man’s head between them. Noemi recalled the kiss the old man had given her. But this would be worse. Howard’s body was covered with boils and he smelled of rot, and he would fruit and he would die. He would die, he would slide into a new body, and Francis would cease to exist. Ademented cycle. Chil- dren devoured as babes, children devoured as adults. Children are but food. Food for a cruel god.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina, softly, quietly, had edged closer to the bed. Her movements had gone unnoticed. All heads were down, after all, all except for Noemi’s.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Then she saw it. Catalina had seized the doctor’s scalpel and was looking at it, very much like a dreamer, very much like someone who does not recognize the object in her hands, still caught in a vague, soporific state.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Then her expression changed. A startled flash of recogni- tion came over her and then a spark of rage. Noemi wasn’t aware that Catalina could be capable of such rage. It was naked hatred, it made Noemi gasp, and at last Howard seemed to notice something amiss and turned his head, only to feel the scalpel descend into his face.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The blow was fierce, going straight into an eye.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina became a maenad, her frenzied stabbing—the scalpel bit the neck, the ear, the shoulder—brought forth a river of black pus and dark blood, splattering the covers. How- ard yelled and shook as if an electric current ran through his body, and the others in the room echoed him, their bodies con- vulsing. The doctor, Florence, Francis, they fell upon the floor, seized by a terrible paroxysm.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_236" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 236 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183529_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina stepped back, dropping the scalpel and slowly moving toward the doorway, where she remained, staring at the room.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi jumped to her feet and rushed to Francis’s side. She could see nothing but the white in his eyes, and she grasped his shoulders and tried to pull him up into a sitting position.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Let’s go!” she said, giving him a hard slap. “Come on, let’s go!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Though dazed, he stood up and clutched her hand, trying to make his way across the room with her. But then Florence’s hand clawed at Noemi’s leg, and she stumbled and lost her footing. Francis tumbled down with her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi attempted to stand again, but there was Florence holding her ankle tight. Noemi saw the gun on the floor and tried to reach for it. Florence, noticing this, leaped upon her like a wild animal, and as Noemi’s fingers closed upon the weapon, Florence’s hand closed around Noemi’s hand, clutch- ing it with a grip so strong Noemi yelped as she heard the cruel, hard cracking of bones.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The pain was atrocious, and her eyes watered as Florence pulled the gun out of her useless hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There’s no way you can leave us,” Florence said. “Ever.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Florence pointed the gun at her, and Noemi knew this bul- let would kill, not wound, for the woman’s face was eager, the mouth a vicious snarl.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They’d cleanse the house afterward, she thought. A mad thought, but it was there, that they’d wash the floors and the linens and scrape off the blood, and toss her into a pit in the cemetery without a cross, like they had so many others.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi raised her injured hand, as if to shield herself, which could do no good. There was no way to dodge a bullet at this range.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No!” Francis yelled.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis lunged toward his mother, and they both crashed against the black velvet chair where Noemi had been sitting, toppling it. There was the noise of the gun going off. It was loud. She pressed her hands against her ears and winced.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She held her breath. Francis lay under the weight of his mother. From the angle where she was sitting Noemi couldn’t see who had been shot, but then Francis rolled Florence away, stood up, and he had the gun in his hands. His eyes were bright with tears and he was shivering, but it wasn’t like the previous monstrous shivers that had wracked his body.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">On the floor, Florence’s body lay still.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_237" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 237 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183534_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He stumbled in Noemi’s direction and shook his head help- lessly. Perhaps he meant to speak, to give himself into the full- ness of grief. But a groan made them both turn their heads to- ward the bed as Howard extended his hands in their direction. He’d lost an eye, and the cuts from the scalpel marred his face. But the other eye remained open and monstrous and golden, staring at them. He spat out blood, spat out black mucus.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're mine. Your body is mine,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He held out his hands, like claws, commanding Francis to approach the bed, and Francis took a step, and Noemi knew in that moment that this compulsion could not abate, that Francis was primed to obey. There was a pull there that could not be ignored. She had assumed, until now, that Ruth had committed suicide, that, horrified by her actions, she’d shot herself.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">I’m not sorry, she’d said, after all. But now Noemi realized it had probably been Howard who had pushed her to do this. He had incited Ruth to turn the rifle onto herself in a last, desper- ate attempt to survive. The Doyles could do such things. They could push you in the desired direction, like Virgil had pushed Noemi.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Ruth, she thought, had been murdered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Now Francis shuffled forward, and Howard grinned. “Come here,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It’s the right time, Noemi thought. A tree ripens and one must pluck the fruit.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was like that, and now Howard was sliding his amber ring off his finger, now he was holding it up for Francis, so that Francis might slide it onto his own hand. A symbol. Of respect, of transference, of acquiescence.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Francis!” she yelled, but he didn’t look at her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Dr. Cummins was moaning. He’d be on his feet any second, and Howard, he was staring at them with that single golden eye, and she needed Francis to turn around and leave. She needed him to step out of there now, because the walls were beginning to palpitate softly all around them, alive, rising and falling, like a great, heaving beast, and the bees had returned.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The maddening movement of a thousand tiny wings.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi leaped forward and dug her nails into Francis’s shoulder.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He turned, he turned and looked at her, and his eyes were fluttering, beginning to roll up.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Francis!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Boy!” Howard yelled. His voice shouldn’t have sounded so loud. It bounced all around them, off the walls, the wood</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_238" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 238 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183539_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">groaning and repeating it while the bees buzzed, their wings flapping in the dark.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Boy boy boy.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It’s in the blood, Ruth had said—but you can cut out a tumor.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis’s fingers were slack around the gun, and Noemi pulled it out of his hand easily. She had shot once in her life before. It had been that trip to El Desierto de los Leones, and her brother had set little target pieces up and their friends had applauded her accurate aim, and then they’d all laughed and gone horseback riding. She remembered the instructions well enough.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi raised the gun and shot Howard twice. Something snapped in Francis. He blinked and stared at Noemi, his mouth open. Then she pulled the trigger again, but she’d run out of bullets.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Howard began to convulse and shriek. Once, when Noemi’s family had gone to the coast on vacation, they’d eaten stew, and she recalled her grandmother slicing the head off a large fish for their dinner with one steady swoop of the knife. The fish had been slippery and fierce, and even after its head had been chopped off it wriggled and attempted to escape. Howard reminded her of the fish, his body rippling violently, wracked with such violence even the bed shook.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi dropped the gun, grabbed Francis by the hand and pulled him out of the room. Catalina was standing in the hall- way, both hands clasped against her mouth, staring at them, staring over Noemi’s shoulder at what lay on the bed, kicking and screaming and dying. Noemi didn’t dare to look back at it.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_239" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 239 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183543_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">hey stopped running when they reached the top</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">of the staircase. Lizzie and Charles, the other two</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">servants at High Place, were standing a few steps</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">below, looking up at them. They shivered, their heads lolling to the side, their hands opening and closing spas- modically, their mouths frozen in a stark grin. It was like ob- serving a couple of wind-up toys that had fallen apart. Noemi guessed that the events that had transpired had affected every family member. It had not, however, destroyed them, for these two were there, staring at them.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What’s wrong with them?” Noemi whispered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Howard lost control of them. They’re stuck. For now. We could attempt to walk past them. But the front entrance might be locked. My mother would have the keys.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We are not headed back for the keys,” Noemi said. She was also unwilling to walk past those two, and she was not heading into Howard’s room again to rifle through a corpse’s pockets.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina moved to stand next to Noemi, staring back at both of the servants, and shook her head. It didn’t look like her cousin was eager to go down the main staircase either.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There’s another way,” Francis said. “We can take the back stairs.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He rushed down a hallway, and the women followed him. “Here,” he said, opening a door.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The back stairs were narrow and the illumination was poor, only a couple of sconces with light bulbs to guide them all the way down. Noemi reached into her pocket and lifted her lighter while holding onto the bannister with her other hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">As they were winding their way down, the bannister seemed to grow slippery under her fingers, like the body of a slick eel. It was alive, it breathed, and rose, and Noemi lowered the lighter and stared at the bannister. Her injured hand was throbbing in unison with the house.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_240" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 240 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183548_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tt’s not real,” Francis said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“But can you see it?” Noemi asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s the gloom. It wants to make us believe things. Go, go quickly.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She walked faster and reached the bottom of the stairs. Catalina came walking right behind her and then Francis, who sounded out of breath.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are you all right?” Noemi asked him.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’m not feeling great,” he said. “We need to keep moving. Ahead it seems to be a dead end; there’s a walk-in pantry and inside it there’s a cupboard. It’s painted yellow. It can be moved aside.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She found a door and inside it the walk-in pantry he had mentioned. The floor was made of stone, and there were hooks to hang meat. A naked light bulb with a long chain dangled from the ceiling. She pulled the chain, illuminating the small space. All the shelves were empty. If this place had stored food, it had been a long time ago, for there was dark mold running up and down the walls, which would have rendered it com- pletely unsuitable for such use.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She saw the yellow cupboard. Its top was arched, and it had two glazed doors and two large drawers at the bottom, marks and scuffs marring its surface. It had been lined in yellow fab- ric, to better match its outside.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We should be able to push it to the left,” Francis said. “And there, in the bottom of the cupboard, there’s a bag.” He still sounded like he was trying to catch his breath.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi bent down and pulled open the cupboard’s bottom drawer. She found a brown canvas bag. Catalina unzipped it for her. Inside of this bag there was an oil lamp, a compass, two sweaters. It was Francis’s unfinished escape kit. It would have to do.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We push it left?” she asked, stuffing the compass in her pocket.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis nodded. “But first, we should block the entrance here,” he said, pointing at the door where they’d come in.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“There’s that bookcase there we can use,” she replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina and Francis proceeded to drag a rickety wooden bookcase against the door. It was not a perfect barricade, but it did the job well enough.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Safely hidden in the small room, Noemi handed one sweater to Catalina and the other to Francis, for it would no doubt be chilly outside. Then it was time to tackle the cup- board. It looked heavy, but surprisingly they were able to</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_241" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 241 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183553_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">slide it to the side with less effort than the bookcase. A dark, weathered door was revealed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It leads to the family crypt,” Francis said. “Then it’s a ques- tion of walking down the mountain to the town.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I don’t want to go there,” Catalina whispered. She had not spoken until now, and the sound startled Noemi. Catalina pointed at the door. “The dead sleep there. I don’t want to go. Listen.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi heard it then, a deep, deep groan. It seemed to make the ceiling above them shiver, and the light bulb flickered, the cord moving a little. A chill went down Noemi’s spine.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What’s that?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis looked up and inhaled. “Howard, he’s alive.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We shot him,” Noemi said. “He’s dead—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No.” Francis shook his head. “He’s weakened and in pain and he’s angry. He’s not dead. The whole house is in pain.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’m scared,” Catalina said, her voice small.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi turned to her cousin and hugged her tight. “We'll soon be out of here, you hear me?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I guess so,” Catalina muttered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi bent down to pick up the oil lamp. Lighting it proved a problem with her injured hand, but she offered Fran- cis her lighter and he helped her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He carefully put the glass chimney back on the oil lamp, glancing at her hand, which she had pressed against her chest. “Want me to hold it?” he asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I can do it,” she told him, because she’d broken two fingers on her left hand, not both her arms, and also because it made her feel safer to carry the lamp.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">With the lamp lit, she turned toward her cousin. Catalina nodded and Noemi smiled. Francis turned the doorknob. A long tunnel stretched out ahead of them. She had expected it to be very rudimentary, the sort of thing the miners might have roughly carved.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was not the case.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The walls had been decorated with yellow tiles, and upon those tiles were painted flower patterns and green, curling vines. On the walls there were graceful silver sconces shaped like snakes. Their open jaws would have held wax candles if they had not been tarnished and covered with dust.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">On the ground and on the walls she noticed a few tiny yellowish mushrooms popping up between stone cracks. It was cold and damp, and no doubt the mushrooms found the conditions underground deeply inviting, for as they advanced they seemed to multiply, clustering together in small clumps.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_242" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 242 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183557_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi began to notice something else as their numbers grew: they seemed to have a glow to them, a vague lumines- cence.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I’m not imagining it, am I?” she asked Francis. “They light up.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes. They do.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s so odd.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s not that unusual. Honey mushrooms and bitter oyster mushrooms both glow. People call it foxfire. But that glow is green.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“These are the mushrooms he found in the cave,” Noemi said, looking up at the ceiling. It was like looking at dozens of tiny stars. “Immortality. In this.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis raised a hand, grasping one of the silver sconces as if to support himself, and looked down at the ground. He ran trembling fingers through his hair and let out a low sigh.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What’s wrong?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s the house. It’s upset and aching. It affects me too.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Can you go on?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T think so,” he said. “I’m not sure. If I faint—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We can stop for a minute,” she offered.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No, it’s fine,” he said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Lean on me. Come on.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You're hurt.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“So are you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He hesitated, but did rest a hand on her shoulder, and they walked together, with Catalina ahead of them. The mush- rooms continued to multiply and grow in size, the soft glow now coming from the ceiling and the walls.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina stopped abruptly. Noemi almost bumped into her and clutched the lamp harder.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What is it?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina raised a hand, pointing ahead. She could see now why her cousin had halted in her steps. The passage- way widened and gave way to two massive double doors of a very dark, very thick wood. Upon the doors was an inlaid silver snake, biting its tail in a perfect circle, and two large door-knockers, twin circles of silver hanging from the jaws of amber-eyed matching snake heads.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It leads to a chamber beneath the crypt,” Francis said. “We must go in there and up.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis pulled one of the door-knockers. The door was heavy, but it yielded after he gave it a harsh tug, and Noemi walked in, her lamp held high. She walked in four paces and</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_243" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 243 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183602_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">lowered the lamp. There was no need for it, no need to light the way.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The chamber was festooned with mushrooms of varying sizes, a living, organic tapestry gracing the walls. They ran up and down the high walls, like barnacles on the hull of an an- cient ship run aground, and they glowed, furnishing the large room with an unwavering source of light, stronger than can- dles or torches. It was the light of a moribund sun.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A metal gate to the right of the chamber had been spared the mushroom growth, and the chandelier above their heads, with its coiling metal snakes and candles reduced to stubs, evidenced no mushrooms either. The stone floor was almost bare of the luxurious mushroom growth, a scant few popping up here and there among loose tiles, and it was easy to see the gigantic mosaic that served as a decoration. It was a black snake, viciously biting its tail, its eyes aglow, and around the reptile there was a curling pattern of vines and flowers. It re- sembled the ouroboros she’d seen in the greenhouse. This one was larger, more magnificent, and the glow of the mushrooms gave it an ominous appearance.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The chamber was bare, except for a table set upon a stone dais. The table was covered with a yellow cloth, and upon it there sat a silver cup and a silver box. Behind the table a long, flowing drape, also of yellow silk, served as a backdrop. It might be a portiere that would hide a doorway.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The gate, it leads up to the mausoleum,” Francis said. “We should take it.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She could indeed see stone steps behind the metal gate, but rather than attempting to open it Noemi walked up to the stone dais, frowning. She set her lamp down on the floor and ran a hand over the table, lifting the lid of the box. Inside she found a knife with a jeweled handle and held it up.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’ve seen this,” she said, “in my dreams.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis and Catalina had slowly walked into the chamber and were both looking at her. “He killed children with this,” she continued.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He did many things,” Francis replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Casual cannibalism.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“A communion. Our children are born infected with the fungus, and ingesting their flesh means ingesting the fungus; ingesting the fungus makes us stronger and in turn it binds us more closely to the gloom. Binds us to Howard.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis winced suddenly and bent down. Noemi thought he was about to retch, but he stood like that, with his arms</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_244" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 244 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183606_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">wrapped around his belly. Noemi dropped the knife on the table and walked down the dais, back to his side.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What is it?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s painful,” he said. “She’s in pain.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Who?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“She’s speaking.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi became aware of a sound. It had been there all along, but she had not paid attention to it. It was very low, al- most inaudible, and it would have been easy to think she was imagining it. It was a hum and also very much unlike a hum. The buzzing noise she’d heard on other occasions, only the pitch this time seemed higher.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Don’t look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi turned around. The hum seemed to be coming from the dais, and she walked up to it. As she moved closer, the hum grew stronger.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was behind the yellow drape. Noemi raised a hand.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t,” Catalina said. “You don’t want to see.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Her fingers touched the drape, and the buzzing became the beating of a thousand frenetic insects against glass, the sound of a swarm caught inside her head, so strong she could almost feel it like a vibration cutting through the air, and she lifted her head.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Don’t look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Bees seemed to flutter against her fingertips, the air alive with unseen wings, and her instinct was to step back, to turn away and shield her eyes, but she clutched the fabric and yanked it aside with such force she almost ripped it to the ground.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi stared straight into the face of death.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was the open, screaming maw of a woman, frozen in time. A mummy, a few teeth dangling from her mouth, her skin yellow. The clothing in which she had been buried had long dissolved into dust, and instead she was clothed in a different finery: mushrooms hid her nakedness. They grew from her torso and her belly, they grew down her arms and her legs, they clustered around her head creating a crown, a halo, of glowing gold. The mushrooms held her upright, an- chored her to the wall, like a monstrous Virgin in a cathedral of mycelium.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And it was this thing, dead and buried for years and years, that made that buzzing noise. It made that terrible sound. This was the golden blur she’d seen in her dreams, the terrify- ing creature that lived in the walls of the house. It held out an</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_245" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 245 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183611_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">outstretched hand, and upon that hand it wore an amber ring. She recognized it.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Agnes,” Noemi said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And the buzzing, it was terrible and sharp and it pulled her down and it made her see and it made her know.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The pressure of the cloth against her face, suffocating her, until she lost consciousness, only to wake up in a coffin. Her startled gasps, because despite being prepared for this, des- pite knowing what must happen, she was scared. Her palms pressing against the lid of the coffin, again and again, splinters digging into her skin, and she screamed, tried to push her way out, but the coffin did not yield. She screamed and screamed but nobody came. Nobody was supposed to come. This was the way it was meant to be.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He needed her. Needed her mind. The fungus by itself, it had no mind. It held no real thoughts, no real consciousness. Faint traces, like the faded scent of roses. Even the cannibaliza- tion of the priests’ remains could not bring true immortality; it augmented the potency of the mushrooms, it created a soft link between all the people present. It united but it could not preserve for all eternity, and the mushrooms themselves could heal, they could extend life, but they could not offer immortal- ity.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Doyle, however, clever, clever Doyle, with his knowledge of scientific and alchemical matters, with his fascination with biological processes, Doyle had understood all the possibilities nobody else had grasped.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A mind.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The fungus needed a human mind that could serve as a vessel for memories, that could offer control. The fungus and the proper human mind, fused together, were like wax, and Howard was like a seal, and he imprinted himself upon new bodies like a seal on paper.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The priests had managed to transmit a few stray memor- ies from one to the other through the mushrooms, through the lineage of their people, but they were crude and random occurrences. Doyle systematized. And all he had needed were people like Agnes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">His wife. His kin.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">But now there was no Agnes. Agnes was the gloom and the gloom was Agnes, and Howard Doyle, if he perished this</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_246" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 246 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183615_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">instant, would still exist in the gloom, for he had created wax and a seal and paper.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And it hurt. It was in pain. The gloom. Agnes. The mush- rooms. The house, heavy with rot, with hidden tendrils ex- tending beneath and up its walls feeding on all manner of dead matter.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He is hurt. We are hurt. Look, look, look. Look!</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The buzzing had acquired a fever pitch; it was so loud Noemi covered her ears and screamed, and inside her head a voice bellowed.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis grabbed her by the shoulders and turned her around.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t look at her,” he said. “We are never supposed to look at her.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The buzzing had ceased all of a sudden, and she raised her head and looked at Catalina, who was staring at the floor, and she looked back at Francis in horror.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">A sob stuck in her throat. “They buried her alive,” Noemi said. “They buried her alive and she died, and the fungus sprouted from her body and...dear God...it’s not a human mind anymore...he remade her. He remade her.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was breathing very fast. Too fast, and the buzzing had ceased, but the woman was still there. Noemi turned her head, tempted to stare again at that hideous skull, but he caught her chin in his hand. “No, no, look at me. Stay here with me.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She took a deep breath, feeling like a diver who was surfacing again. Noemi stared into Francis’s eyes. “She’s the gloom. Did you know?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Only Howard and Virgil come here,” Francis said. He was shivering.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“But you knew!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">All the ghosts were Agnes. Or rather, all the ghosts lived inside Agnes. No, that wasn’t right either. What had once been Agnes had become the gloom, and inside the gloom there lived ghosts. It was maddening. It was not a haunting. It was pos- session and not even that, but something she couldn’t even begin to describe. The creation of an afterlife, furnished with the marrow and the bones and the neurons of a woman, made of stems and spores.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Ruth knew too, and we couldn’t do anything. She keeps us here, she’s how Howard controls everything. We can’t leave. They don’t let us, ever.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">He was sweating and then he was sliding down onto his knees, grasping Noemi’s arms. “What is it? You must get up,” she said, also sliding to her knees, touching his face.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_247" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 247 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183620_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He's right, he can’t leave. Neither can you, for that matter.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">It was Virgil who had spoken. He was swinging the metal gate open and walking in. Strolling in. Very casually. Perhaps he was a hallucination. Perhaps he wasn’t even there. Noemi stared at him. It can’t be, she thought.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“What?” he said with a shrug, letting the gate close behind him with a loud clang. He was there. It was no hallucination. Rather than following them down the tunnel he had simply gone aboveground, through the cemetery, and descended the steps from the crypt.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Poor girl. You actually look shocked. You didn’t really think you had killed me. You also didn’t think I accidentally happened to carry that tincture in my pocket, did you? I let you have it, Ilet you snap out of our hold for a few moments. I let you cause this mayhem.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She swallowed. Next to her Francis was shivering. “Why?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Isn't it obvious? So you could hurt my father. I couldn’t. Francis couldn't. The old man ensured none of us could raise a hand against him. You saw how he forced Ruth to kill herself. When I learned what Francis was up to I thought: here’s my chance. Let the girl escape her bonds, let’s see what she can do, this outsider who isn’t subject to our rules quite yet, who can still fight back. And now he’s dying. Feel it? Hmm? His body is falling apart.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“That can’t be good for you,” Noemi said. “If you hurt him, you hurt the gloom, and besides, even if his body dies, he’ll still exist in the gloom. His mind—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“He’s weakened. I control the gloom now,” Virgil said an- grily. “When he dies, he’ll die forever. I won't let him have a new body. Change. That’s what you wanted, no? Turns out we want the same.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil had reached Catalina’s side and was glancing at her with a smirk. “There you are, dear wife. Thank you for your contribution to the evening’s entertainment,” he said, squeez- ing her arm in a gesture of mock affection. Catalina winced, but did not move.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t touch her,” Noemi said, standing up and reaching for the knife in the silver box.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t be meddlesome. She’s my wife.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi closed her fingers around the knife. “You better not</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“You better drop that knife,” Virgil replied.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Never, she thought, yet her hand was shaking and there was this terrible impulse rushing through her body, pushing her to obey.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_248" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 248 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183625_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I drank the tincture. You can’t control me.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Funny, that,” Virgil said, letting go of Catalina and looking at Noemi. “You did snap out of our control back there. But the tincture doesn’t seem to last that long, and walking all around the house, down into this chamber, you've been exposed tothe gloom’s influence again. You're breathing it in, all these tiny, invisible spores. You're in the heart of the house. All three of you.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The gloom is hurt. You can’t—”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“We've all taken a beating today,” Virgil said, and she could now see there were beads of sweat dotting his forehead and his blue eyes had a feverish sheen to them. “But I’m in control now, and youre going to do as I say.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Her fingers ached, and suddenly it felt like she was holding a hot coal in her hand. Noemi let the knife fall to the floor with aloud clang and a yelp.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Told you,” Virgil said mockingly.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She looked down at the knife, which lay by her foot. It was so close, yet she could not pick it up. She felt pins and needles running down her arms, making her fingers twitch. Her hand hurt, the broken bones ached with a terrible, burning pain.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Look at this place,” Virgil said, glancing at the chandelier above their heads with distaste. “Howard was caught in the past, but I look forward to the future. We’ll have to reopen the mine, see about getting new furniture in here, real elec- tric power. We'll need servants, of course, new automobiles, and children. I expect you'll have no problem giving me many children.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“No,” she said, but it was a whisper, and she could sense his grip on her, like an invisible hand settling on her shoulder.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Come here,” Virgil ordered. “You’ve been mine since the beginning.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The mushrooms on the walls swayed, as if they were alive, like anemones rippling under water. They released clouds of golden dust and they sighed. Or it was she who sighed, for there was that sweet, dark feeling she had felt before envelop- ing her once again, and she was suddenly light-headed. The troublesome pain of her left hand lifted and vanished.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil was holding his arms out to her, and Noemi thought of those arms twined around her and how good it would be to surrender to his will. Deep down she wished to be torn apart, to scream in shame; his palm muffling that scream against her mouth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The mushrooms glowed brighter, and she thought perhaps later she might touch them, running her hands against the</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_249" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 249 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183629_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">wall and settling her face against the softness of their flesh. It would be good to rest there, skin pressed tight against their slick bodies, and maybe they’d cover her, the lovely fungi, and cram into her mouth, into her nostrils and eye sockets until she could not breathe and they nestled in her belly and bloomed along her thighs. And Virgil, too, driving deep within her, and the world would be a blur of gold.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t,” Francis said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She had taken one step down the dais, but Francis had reached out a hand and clasped her injured fingers, the pain of his touch making her wince. She looked down at him, blink- ing, and froze.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t,” he whispered, and she could tell he was afraid. Nevertheless, he descended the steps ahead of her, as if he might shield her. His voice sounded frail and strained, ready to splinter. “Let them go.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Why would I ever do that?” Virgil asked innocently.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s wrong. Everything we do is wrong.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil pointed over his shoulder, toward the tunnel they had followed. “Hear that? That’s my father dying, and when his body finally collapses I will have absolute power over the gloom. I’ll need an ally. We are kin, after all.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi thought that she could indeed hear something, that in the distance Howard Doyle groaned and spat blood, and black fluid leaked from his body as he strained to keep breath- ing.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Look, Francis, I’m not a selfish man. We can share,” Virgil said expansively. “You want the girl, I want the girl. It’s no reason to fight, huh? And Catalina is a sweet thing too. Come, come, don’t be dull.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis had picked up the knife she had dropped, and now he held it up. “You won't hurt them.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are you going to try and stab me? I should warn you l’ma little harder to kill than a woman. Yes, Francis, you managed to kill your mother. Over what? A girl? And now? It’s my turn?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Go to hell!”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis rushed toward Virgil, but he suddenly halted, his hand frozen in midair, the knife tight in his grip. Noemi couldn't see his face but she could imagine it. It must mirror her expression, for she too had become a statue, and Catalina stood in absolute stillness.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The bees stirred, the buzzing began. Look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Don’t make me kill you,” Virgil warned him, and his hand fell upon Francis’s trembling hand. “Yield.”</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_250" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 250 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183634_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis shoved Virgil away, sending Virgil crashing against the wall with a strength that seemed impossible.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">For one split second she felt Virgil’s pain, the tug of adren- aline rushing through her veins, his fury mingled with her own. Francis, you little shit. It was the gloom, connecting them for a brief instant, and she yelped, almost biting her tongue. She stepped back, her feet slowly obeying her. One, two steps.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil frowned. His eyes seemed to glow gold as he stepped forward and brushed off tiny bits of mushrooms and dust that had adhered to his jacket.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The buzzing bubbled up, first low, then rumbling into life, and she winced.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yield.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Francis groaned his answer and flung himself against Vir- gil once more. His cousin stopped him with ease. He was much stronger, and this time he was prepared for an attack. He caught Francis’s desperate punch, returning it with vicious abandon, hitting Francis in the head. Francis stumbled yet managed to regain his balance and struck back. His fist con- nected with Virgil’s mouth, and Virgil let out an angry, startled gasp.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil’s eyes narrowed as he wiped his mouth clean.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“T’ll make you bite off your own tongue,” Virgil said simply.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The men had changed positions, and now Noemi could see Francis’s face, the blood welling down his temple as he heaved and shook his head, and Noemi saw the way his eyes were open wide and the way his hands were shaking and how his mouth was opening and closing, like a fish gasping for air.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Dear God, Virgil was going to make him do it. He would make him eat his own tongue.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi heard the growing buzzing of bees behind her.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Look.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She turned around, and her eyes fell on the face of Agnes, her lipless mouth set in an eternal circle of pain, and she pressed her hands against her ears, furiously wondering why it wouldn’t stop. Why that noise wouldn’t cease, returning over and over again.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">And it struck her all of a sudden this fact that she had missed, which should have been obvious from the very begin- ning: that the frightening and twisted gloom that surrounded them was the manifestation of all the suffering that had been inflicted on this woman. Agnes. Driven to madness, driven to anger, driven to despair, and even now a sliver of that woman remained, and that sliver was still screaming in agony.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was the snake biting its tail.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_251" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 251 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183638_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She was a dreamer, eternally bound to a nightmare, eyes closed even when her eyes had turned to dust.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The buzzing was her voice. She could not communicate properly any longer but could still scream of unspeakable hor- rors inflicted on her, of ruin and pain. Even when coherent memory and thought had been scraped away, this searing rage remained, burning the minds of any who wandered near it. What did she wish?</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Simply to be released from this torment.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Simply to wake up. But she couldn’t. She couldn’t ever wake.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The buzzing was growing, threatening to hurt Noemi again and overwhelm her mind, but she reached down and grabbed the oil lamp with quick, rough fingers and rather than think- ing about what she was about to do, she thought of that single phrase that Ruth had spoken. Open your eyes, open your eyes, and her steps were quick and determined, and for each step she whispered open your eyes.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Until she was staring at Agnes again.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Sleepwalker,” she whispered. “Time to open your eyes.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She tossed the lamp against the corpse’s face. It instantly ignited the mushrooms around Agnes’s head, creating a halo of fire, and then tongues of fire began to spread quickly down the wall, the organic matter apparently as good as kindling, making the mushrooms blacken and pop.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil screamed. It was a hoarse, terrible scream, and he collapsed upon the floor and scratched at the tiles, attempt- ing to stand up. Francis also collapsed. Agnes was the gloom and the gloom was part of them, and this sudden damage to Agnes, to the web of mushrooms, must be like neurons ignit- ing. Noemi for her part felt jolted into complete awareness, the gloom shoving her away.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She rushed down the dais and immediately went to her cousin, pressing her hand against Catalina’s face.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Are you all right?” she asked.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes,” Catalina said, nodding vigorously. “Yes.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">On the floor, both Virgil and Francis were moaning. Virgil tried to reach for her, tried to lift himself up, and Noemi kicked him in the face, but he clawed at her, scrabbled to grab hold of her leg. Noemi stepped back, and he was extending a hand, still grasping and pulling himself forward even though he couldn’t walk. He crawled toward her, gritting his teeth.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Noemi took another step back, fearing he’d pounce upon her.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_252" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 252 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183643_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Catalina picked up the knife Francis had dropped, and now she stood over her husband and brought the knife down into his face when he turned to look at her, piercing an eye, in imi- tation of what she’d done to Howard Doyle.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Virgil fell down with a muffled groan, and Catalina pressed the knife in deeper, her lips closed together, not a single word or sob escaping them. Virgil twitched and his mouth fell open, spitting and gasping. Then he lay still.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The women held hands and looked down at Virgil. His blood was smearing the black head of the snake, painting it red, and Noemi wished they’d had a great big knife, for she would have cut off his head if she could, like her grandmother had cut off the head of the fish.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">She knew, by the way Catalina clutched her hand, that she wished for the same.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Then Francis muttered a word, and Noemi knelt next to him and tried to get him to stand up. “Come on,” she told him, “we need to run.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“It’s dying, we are dying,” Francis said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes, we are going to die if we don’t get out quickly,” Noemi agreed. The whole room was quickly catching on fire, patches and patches of mushrooms bursting into flames, and the yellow curtains she had pulled aside were also burning.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“I can’t leave.”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Yes, you can,” Noemi said, gritting her teeth and coaxing him to his feet. She couldn’t make him walk, though.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Catalina, help us!” she yelled.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">They each took one of Francis’s arms and placed it over their shoulders, half lifting, half dragging him toward the metal gate. It was easy to swing it open, but then Noemi eyed the steps leading up and wondered how they were going to manage that climb. But there was no other way. When she looked back, she saw Virgil on the ground, stray sparks falling upon him, and the chamber burning bright. There were also mushrooms growing on the walls of the staircase, and these too seemed to be catching fire. They had to hurry.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Up they went, as fast as they could, and Noemi pinched Francis to get him to open his eyes and assist them. He man- aged to climb several steps with their aid before Noemi was forced to literally drag him up the last couple of steps, stum- bling into a dusty chamber with crypts running from one side to the other. Noemi glimpsed silver plaques, rotting coffins, empty vases that might once have contained flowers, a few of the little glowing mushrooms upon the ground, providing the faintest illumination.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="book-page" id="gothic_page_253" style="margin-bottom:2rem; padding-bottom:1.5rem; border-bottom:1px dashed #e2e8f0;">
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem; text-transform:uppercase; letter-spacing:1px; color:#718096; margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Page 253 &bull; Screenshot_20261018_183648_Kindle.jpg</div>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">The door leading to the mausoleum was mercifully open, courtesy of Virgil. When they stepped out, the mist and the night were waiting to embrace them.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“The gate,” she told Catalina, “do you know the way to the gate?”</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Tt’s too dark, the mist,” her cousin said.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">Yes, the mist that had frightened Noemi with its mysteri- ous golden blur, that buzzing that had been Agnes. But Agnes was a pillar of fire beneath their feet now, and they must find their way out of this place.</p>
      <p style="margin-bottom:0.9rem; text-indent:1.5rem;">“Francis, you need to guide us to the gate,” Noemi said.
